Bedrock's EU profile does not route EU-originated requests to London, and London-originated requests can route to EU regions [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S005: Geographic cross-Region inference - Amazon Bedrock (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/geographic-cross-region-inference.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. A firm that needs UK-only processing has to check per model whether an in-region London option exists [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Overflow is a residency decision.** On Vertex AI, traffic above a Provisioned Throughput quota goes to the global endpoint by default unless overridden [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S008: Data residency (with Deployments and endpoints; Single Zone Provisioned Throughput) - Vertex AI (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/learn/data-residency> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. The Single Zone variant keeps overflow in the purchased region, but is excluded from the Gemini online inference SLA [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S008: Data residency (with Deployments and endpoints; Single Zone Provisioned Throughput) - Vertex AI (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/learn/data-residency> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. For each route, decide which property matters more, residency or the SLA, and write it down [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Defaults, not just options.** Provider defaults differ. Together AI stores prompts and responses unless zero data retention is switched on [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S130: Zero data retention / Privacy and security - Together AI docs (and EU data centers support article) <https://docs.together.ai/docs/zero-data-retention> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Fireworks has zero data retention on by default for open models, except its Response API, which stores conversations for 30 days unless told not to [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S134: Zero Data Retention / Data residency / Regions - Fireworks AI Docs <https://docs.fireworks.ai/guides/security_compliance/data_handling> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Record the retention setting per route [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Capacity is a resilience issue.** Serverless per-token tiers carry no reserved capacity, and Together AI's serverless tier has no SLA, while its provisioned tier does [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S131: Pricing - Together AI docs (serverless, dedicated endpoints, GPU clusters) <https://docs.together.ai/docs/inference/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. All three hyperscalers sell reserved or provisioned capacity [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S006: Understanding deployment types in Microsoft Foundry Models <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/foundry-models/concepts/deployment-types> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; B-L2-S008: Data residency (with Deployments and endpoints; Single Zone Provisioned Throughput) - Vertex AI (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/learn/data-residency> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; B-L2-S005: Geographic cross-Region inference - Amazon Bedrock (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/geographic-cross-region-inference.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. In the worked example, every fund's draft is requested in a narrow window after the attribution runs complete, so capacity must be sized for that peak, rehearsed in the month before go-live, and backed by batch processing for non-urgent regeneration [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Why managed access is the default.** The DORA critical-provider list and the UK critical third-party designations include AWS, Google Cloud and Microsoft, and no AI model provider [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DORA: Regulation (EU) 2022/2554 Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S021: Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) pages and RoI reporting FAQ (ESMA / EBA / EIOPA) <https://www.esma.europa.eu/esmas-activities/digital-finance-and-innovation/digital-operational-resilience-act-dora> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; R-UK-CTP: UK Critical Third Parties regime (FSMA 2023 / FSMA 2000 s312L; PRA/FCA/BoE CTP rules) and the Critical Third Parties (Designation) Regulations 2026 (SI 2026/777) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S023: The Critical Third Parties (Designation) Regulations 2026 (SI 2026/777); BoE/FCA/HMT announcements (HM Treasury / legislation.gov.uk / Bank of England) <https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2026/777/made> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Consuming a model through the primary cloud therefore places inference inside an already-overseen, already-contracted relationship; a direct contract with an inference cloud or router does not [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Ownership is also moving among access providers: Stripe agreed to acquire OpenRouter, with closing pending [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — V1-S059: Stripe agrees to acquire OpenRouter to help businesses optimize token routing and usage <https://stripe.com/newsroom/news/stripe-agrees-to-acquire-openrouter> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; V1-S060: Stripe to buy OpenRouter as fintech expands deeper into AI (and TechCrunch 16 Aug 2026 report) (CNBC / TechCrunch) <https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/19/stripe-openrouter-fintech-ai-model-marketplace-.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. From 18 March 2027 a new material provider needs UK notification lead time [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); R-FCA-SYSC8: FCA Handbook SYSC 8 (Outsourcing); FG16/5 'Guidance for firms outsourcing to the cloud and other third-party IT services'; PS26/2 operational incident and third-party reporting (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S062: PS7/26 Operational incident and third-party reporting (PRA) and FCA PS26/2; revised SS2/21 (March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA; FCA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/prudential-regulation/publication/2026/march/operational-incident-and-third-party-reporting-policy-statement> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].

**The stressed exit.** PRA SS2/21 expects documented and tested exit plans, including a stressed exit [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S048: SS2/21 Outsourcing and third party risk management (and PS7/26, March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/-/media/boe/files/prudential-regulation/supervisory-statement/2021/ss221-march-21.pdf> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. In this layer, the credible stressed-exit route is an open-weight model already qualified on a portable engine such as vLLM, switched by gateway configuration [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. An exit plan that names a second provider but has never been drilled is not tested [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Capacity plans are rarely celebrated. The site reliability engineers who see the month-end peak coming deserve more credit than they usually get.

### What good looks like

The signal is **capacity headroom at peak**: reserved or provisioned throughput minus observed demand in the busiest window, for this example the month-end hours when every fund's draft is requested at once [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Measure it by comparing the capacity plan with peak-hour metrics from the gateway, and rehearse the peak before go-live rather than discovering it in production [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Re-measure after each month-end, because the number of funds, and so the peak, tends to grow faster than the reservation [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

The review's target guidance is positive headroom at the forecast peak plus a margin, before go-live [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Three companions complete the picture. The throttle rate on reserved routes should be near zero, with an alert on any sustained rate [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The in-region processing rate for client-data routes should be 100%, evidenced per call from records such as Bedrock's processing-region field [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S005: Geographic cross-Region inference - Amazon Bedrock (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/geographic-cross-region-inference.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. And the exit drill, moving a route to the qualified alternative, should take hours, by configuration only [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. These are a starting point, not a benchmark [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 3: model access.** Drafting through the primary cloud's in-region model service, with capacity sized for the month-end peak and an open-weight route kept ready as the stressed exit. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** Every fund's draft can be produced in the narrow month-end window. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** No router or provider that cannot guarantee region and retention. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** Endpoint, region, retention setting and fallback flag per call. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Capacity headroom at the month-end peak. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"At our volume, self-hosting is simply cheaper."** It may be. But compare like with like. Pricing units differ: per million tokens, per replica-minute, per GPU-second and per instance-minute [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S131: Pricing - Together AI docs (serverless, dedicated endpoints, GPU clusters) <https://docs.together.ai/docs/inference/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S135: Fireworks - Pricing (and Serverless Pricing docs) (Fireworks AI) <https://fireworks.ai/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S139: Pricing - Build with Cerebras Inference (Cerebras Systems) <https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/support/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S083: Inference Endpoints Pricing (Hugging Face) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/hf-endpoints-documentation/main/docs/source/support/pricing.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Model the cost at forecast utilisation, including idle reserved capacity and the staff who patch and run the engines [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. If the case still holds, self-host. Just do it for capacity, residency or exit reasons you can name [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Managed access just moves our concentration to one cloud."** It does, and the post says so [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The concentration runs deeper still: most self-hosted and many managed routes depend on one GPU vendor [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S090: NVIDIA Dynamo README (GitHub) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-dynamo/dynamo/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S132: Together AI Raises $800 Million at $8.3 Billion Valuation (press release, 1 Jul 2026; TechCrunch coverage) (Together AI (Business Wire)) <https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260701243402/en/Together-AI-Raises-00-Million-at-.3-Billion-Valuation-to-Make-Frontier-AI-Accessible-to-All> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S141: Fireworks Raises a $1.5 Billion Series D (press release; CNBC 16 Jul 2026) (Fireworks AI (Business Wire)) <https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260716264405/en/Fireworks-Raises-a-$1.5-Billion-Series-D-to-Lead-the-Specialized-Intelligence-Revolution> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The answer is a small portfolio, the primary cloud's service plus one qualified second route, not running everything in-house [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Retreating into a private estate swaps a concentration the supervisors already oversee for operational risks the firm carries alone [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"A router gives us every provider through one contract."** Convenient, but it puts a third party between the firm and its model providers, and adds fees: OpenRouter charges 5.5% on credit purchases [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — V1-S062: Simplifying Our Platform Fee (OpenRouter) <https://openrouter.ai/blog/announcements/simplifying-our-platform-fee/> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Use a router only behind the firm's gateway, never as the gateway, and never for client data without contractual zero retention and residency [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. If a router is used, it should fail rather than fall back out of region; OpenRouter's in-region routing behaves that way [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S109: OpenRouter Docs: In-Region Routing (EU/US) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenRouterTeam/docs/main/guides/features/in-region-routing.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S150: In-Region Routing: keep your data in the US or EU (OpenRouter blog) and OpenRouter Pricing/Enterprise pages <https://openrouter.ai/blog/announcements/us-in-region-routing/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Every router or inference cloud used for a function is also an ICT third-party arrangement for the DORA register [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DORA: Regulation (EU) 2022/2554 Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S021: Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) pages and RoI reporting FAQ (ESMA / EBA / EIOPA) <https://www.esma.europa.eu/esmas-activities/digital-finance-and-innovation/digital-operational-resilience-act-dora> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].

### Questions for your team

- For each production route, where are prompts processed, as distinct from stored, and how do we evidence it per call? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- What happens to traffic above our reserved capacity, and has anyone checked the default? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- What was our peak demand in the last month-end window, against what we have reserved? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Who patches our inference engines, and how many days does a critical advisory take to reach production? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Is there an open-weight route already qualified for the stressed-exit case, and when was it last drilled? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Which provider defaults, such as data retention, have we accepted without checking? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### In one line

The cheapest token is worth nothing if it arrives late or is processed in the wrong country [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.


## 4. The boring component that makes exit real

*C1 AI / LLM gateway, from Post 4 of the series.*

### The post

The most boring component in the AI stack is the one that makes an exit plan real. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

UK supervisors expect documented and tested exit plans for material outsourcing, including a stressed exit. Without a gateway, provider SDKs and keys spread through application code, and switching model becomes a programme of code changes. The plan exists on paper; it cannot be carried out in the time a stressed exit allows. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S048: SS2/21 Outsourcing and third party risk management (and PS7/26, March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/-/media/boe/files/prudential-regulation/supervisory-statement/2021/ss221-march-21.pdf> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Managed model access is a sensible default only if it stays reversible. Every model call, and now every tool and agent call, passes through one firm-controlled point that owns routing, fallback, budgets, policy and the request log. Guardrails, data protection, identity checks and cost attribution are all invoked there. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S015: LiteLLM docs: MCP Overview, Agent Gateway (A2A), Caching, Budgets/Rate Limits, v1.89.0 release notes (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/mcp> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S016: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA — And It's Already Moving Faster (Kong Inc.) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S053: AI gateway capabilities in Azure API Management (Microsoft Learn source, ms.date 29 May 2026) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs/main/articles/api-management/genai-gateway-capabilities.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S024: Apigee AI solutions: gateway to enterprise-ready AI (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/solutions/apigee-ai> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S061: agentgateway README (GitHub) (agentgateway project (Linux Foundation)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/agentgateway/agentgateway/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

That concentration cuts both ways. In March 2026, malicious releases of a popular open-source gateway were published to a public package index. A component that holds every provider key is a prime target. So run it like payments infrastructure: two independent deployments, pinned and signed builds, and budgets, guards and fallbacks that fail closed. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S008: Security Update: Suspected Supply Chain Incident (and Security Townhall Updates) (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/blog/security-update-march-2026> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S027: LiteLLM PyPI supply chain attack (with NetSPI, Comet, Snyk/Datadog reporting) (FutureSearch) <https://futuresearch.ai/blog/litellm-pypi-supply-chain-attack/> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]] [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The signal is time to switch provider: how long it takes to move a route to a pre-qualified alternative by configuration alone. Hours, not weeks, and drilled at least once a year. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The leadership move is to treat "add a provider" as a third-party risk event, not a configuration tweak. From 18 March 2027, UK firms must notify material third-party arrangements before entering them. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); R-FCA-SYSC8: FCA Handbook SYSC 8 (Outsourcing); FG16/5 'Guidance for firms outsourcing to the cloud and other third-party IT services'; PS26/2 operational incident and third-party reporting (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S062: PS7/26 Operational incident and third-party reporting (PRA) and FCA PS26/2; revised SS2/21 (March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA; FCA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/prudential-regulation/publication/2026/march/operational-incident-and-third-party-reporting-policy-statement> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The honest caveat: ownership matters. Several gateways now belong to security, payments or platform companies, and a gateway that is not neutral weakens the one component meant to keep you neutral. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S011: Palo Alto Networks Completes Acquisition of Portkey to Secure AI Agents (and 30 Apr 2026 announcement) <https://www.paloaltonetworks.com/company/press/2026/palo-alto-networks-completes-acquisition-of-portkey-to-secure-ai-agents> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S025: Palo Alto Networks Completes Acquisition of Portkey to Secure AI Agents (with PANW FY2026 10-K and 10-Q, SDxCentral) <https://www.paloaltonetworks.com/company/press/2026/palo-alto-networks-completes-acquisition-of-portkey-to-secure-ai-agents> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; V1-S059: Stripe agrees to acquire OpenRouter to help businesses optimize token routing and usage <https://stripe.com/newsroom/news/stripe-agrees-to-acquire-openrouter> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Neutrality is a design property. It has to be built, and then rehearsed. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

![The boring component that makes exit real](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P04.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 4. Every model, tool and agent call passes through one firm-controlled gateway, so moving to a pre-qualified alternative is a configuration change, not a code programme. Generic: no vendor names.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

The popular picture of the stack has no gateway. Its nearest equivalent is a hosted router drawn inside the inference layer [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. That absence matters, because the gateway turned out to be the component the rest of the architecture depends on.

**What the gateway decides.** For every outbound AI request, the gateway decides who may make it, which model or tool receives it, in which region, at what cost ceiling, under which policies, and what record is kept [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. In practice that means one OpenAI-compatible API in front of many providers, so applications hold no provider SDKs or keys; routing, retry and fallback; token and spend limits per key, team or user; caching; inline calls to guardrails, data protection and authorisation; and one log record per request [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S015: LiteLLM docs: MCP Overview, Agent Gateway (A2A), Caching, Budgets/Rate Limits, v1.89.0 release notes (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/mcp> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S049: Portkey AI Gateway README (GitHub) (Portkey (Palo Alto Networks)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Portkey-AI/gateway/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S063: Envoy AI Gateway / Agent Router README (GitHub) (Envoy AI Gateway project (Agentic AI Foundation)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/envoyproxy/ai-gateway/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S024: Apigee AI solutions: gateway to enterprise-ready AI (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/solutions/apigee-ai> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S020: Azure API Management AI gateway docs and Tech Community announcements (AI Gateway tier preview; Build 2026 updates) (Microsoft) <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/api-management/ai-gateway-overview> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S105: AgentCore Gateway: Inference targets; Inference connector/provider targets; Add rate limits to a gateway (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway-targets-inference.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Why exit depends on it.** PRA SS2/21 expects documented and tested exit plans, including a stressed exit [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S048: SS2/21 Outsourcing and third party risk management (and PS7/26, March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/-/media/boe/files/prudential-regulation/supervisory-statement/2021/ss221-march-21.pdf> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Without a gateway, provider SDKs and keys spread through application code, so switching model becomes a programme of code changes that cannot be executed in the time a stressed exit allows [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. With one, a model exit is a configuration change, re-qualified by the evaluation suite described in Chapter 1 [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. An exit plan that has never been drilled through the gateway is not tested [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**It now carries three kinds of traffic.** The most significant change during 2025 and 2026 is scope. LiteLLM, Kong, Azure API Management, Apigee and agentgateway all now govern model calls, tool calls over the Model Context Protocol (MCP) and agent-to-agent (A2A) calls [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S015: LiteLLM docs: MCP Overview, Agent Gateway (A2A), Caching, Budgets/Rate Limits, v1.89.0 release notes (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/mcp> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S016: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA — And It's Already Moving Faster (Kong Inc.) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S053: AI gateway capabilities in Azure API Management (Microsoft Learn source, ms.date 29 May 2026) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs/main/articles/api-management/genai-gateway-capabilities.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S024: Apigee AI solutions: gateway to enterprise-ready AI (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/solutions/apigee-ai> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S061: agentgateway README (GitHub) (agentgateway project (Linux Foundation)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/agentgateway/agentgateway/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. MCP originated at Anthropic and was donated to the Agentic AI Foundation under the Linux Foundation; the independent alternative is OpenAPI-described tools behind the same gateway [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A3-S018: MCP joins the Agentic AI Foundation (9 Dec 2025) (Model Context Protocol project (blog)) <https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2025-12-09-mcp-joins-agentic-ai-foundation/> accessed 2026-10-07; V1-S038: Linux Foundation Announces the Formation of the Agentic AI Foundation (AAIF) (The Linux Foundation) <https://www.linuxfoundation.org/press/linux-foundation-announces-the-formation-of-the-agentic-ai-foundation> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. For tool traffic the gateway becomes a federator, exposing only the tools each caller may use [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S016: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA — And It's Already Moving Faster (Kong Inc.) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S026: Policy in Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is now generally available; AgentCore Policy docs; Why Policy in AgentCore chose Cedar (Amazon Web Services) <https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/03/policy-amazon-bedrock-agentcore-generally-available/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The MCP 2026-07-28 authorisation specification forbids token passthrough, so a gateway in front of tool servers must exchange tokens rather than forward the caller's own [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S033: MCP specification 2026-07-28: Authorization (Model Context Protocol project) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/main/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S034: MCP specification 2026-07-28: Authorization security considerations (Model Context Protocol project) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/main/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The other controls are invoked here too: guardrails, data protection, tool policy and cost attribution all run inline at the gateway [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S017: Kong AI Gateway changelog; Kong Gateway changelog; AI Proxy Advanced plugin docs (Kong Inc.) <https://developer.konghq.com/ai-gateway/changelog/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S053: AI gateway capabilities in Azure API Management (Microsoft Learn source, ms.date 29 May 2026) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs/main/articles/api-management/genai-gateway-capabilities.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S026: Policy in Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is now generally available; AgentCore Policy docs; Why Policy in AgentCore chose Cedar (Amazon Web Services) <https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/03/policy-amazon-bedrock-agentcore-generally-available/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A7-S010: litellm on PyPI (JSON API) (PyPI / BerriAI) <https://pypi.org/pypi/litellm/json> accessed 2026-10-07]. That is why the review moved the gateway out of the inference layer and into the control plane as an "AI traffic gateway" [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The concentration cuts both ways.** A component that holds every provider credential and sees every prompt is a high-value target [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. On 24 March 2026, malicious LiteLLM releases 1.82.7 and 1.82.8 were published to PyPI after release credentials were stolen through a compromised CI scanner [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S008: Security Update: Suspected Supply Chain Incident (and Security Townhall Updates) (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/blog/security-update-march-2026> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S010: Your AI Gateway Was a Backdoor: Inside the LiteLLM Supply Chain Compromise (Trend Micro Research) <https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/research/26/c/inside-litellm-supply-chain-compromise.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. A clean 1.83.0 followed, and the container images have been cosign-signed from that version [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S009: v1.83.0 - Official Release (Post Supply Chain Incident) (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/release_notes/v1.83.0/v1-83-0> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S051: LiteLLM README (GitHub) (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/BerriAI/litellm/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. The lesson is not about one project. Any gateway, open or closed, concentrates risk in the way it concentrates control [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Run it like payments infrastructure: at least two independent deployments, builds installed from pinned and verified artefacts, and a defined degraded mode, preferably "no AI" for regulated outputs and never a direct path for client data [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Fail closed, explicitly.** Defaults deserve suspicion. In LiteLLM, limits are unset by default, `max_budget` fails open without a database, prompt-injection guardrails are off by default, and the proxy admin key bypasses budget checks [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S015: LiteLLM docs: MCP Overview, Agent Gateway (A2A), Caching, Budgets/Rate Limits, v1.89.0 release notes (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/mcp> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Every product has its equivalents, so test each failure mode rather than trusting the configuration screen [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Caches deserve the same care. Apigee shipped a fix for a server-side request forgery flaw in its semantic-cache lookup on 30 September 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S025: Apigee release notes (all; hybrid) (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/apigee/docs/release-notes> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. For client-specific routes, turn semantic caching off [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Fallback must respect residency and qualification.** A fallback model must be in the same approved region and must have passed the route's regression suite [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. "Fall back to anything available" is how out-of-region processing happens on a busy month-end [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The market is real but uneven.** Kong AI Gateway 2.0 reached general availability on 1 September 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S016: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA — And It's Already Moving Faster (Kong Inc.) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S034: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA (with Kong press release 30 Sep 2026 on AI Gateway 2.2 GA) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Apigee's MCP support has been generally available since 31 March 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S024: Apigee AI solutions: gateway to enterprise-ready AI (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/solutions/apigee-ai> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S023: Apigee docs: AI gateway capabilities; semantic caching tutorial; MCP support for Apigee (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/apigee/docs/api-platform/get-started/ai-capabilities> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The Azure API Management AI Gateway tier is in preview with no SLA, while its AI policies are generally available [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S020: Azure API Management AI gateway docs and Tech Community announcements (AI Gateway tier preview; Build 2026 updates) (Microsoft) <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/api-management/ai-gateway-overview> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S073: AI Gateway tier (preview) overview - Azure API Management (with Tech Community 'AI Gateway tier of API Management now in public preview') (Microsoft) <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/api-management/ai-gateway-overview> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. No AWS product called "AI gateway" was found; AWS offers AgentCore Gateway for tools, with new inference targets, and a LiteLLM-based reference pattern [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S021: Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Pricing; AgentCore GA announcement (Oct 2025); AgentCore Gateway docs (Amazon Web Services) <https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/pricing/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S022: Guidance for Multi-Provider Generative AI Gateway on AWS; Implementing resilience patterns with Amazon Bedrock and LLM gateway (Amazon Web Services) <https://aws.amazon.com/solutions/guidance/multi-provider-generative-ai-gateway-on-aws/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S105: AgentCore Gateway: Inference targets; Inference connector/provider targets; Add rate limits to a gateway (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway-targets-inference.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Microsoft describes its AI gateway as an extension of the API gateway, "not a separate offering" [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S053: AI gateway capabilities in Azure API Management (Microsoft Learn source, ms.date 29 May 2026) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs/main/articles/api-management/genai-gateway-capabilities.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. The review therefore draws the gateway as a logical control that may live on the firm's existing API-management platform [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Ownership is the caveat.** Palo Alto Networks completed its acquisition of Portkey on 29 May 2026 and sells it as Prisma AIRS AI Gateway [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S011: Palo Alto Networks Completes Acquisition of Portkey to Secure AI Agents (and 30 Apr 2026 announcement) <https://www.paloaltonetworks.com/company/press/2026/palo-alto-networks-completes-acquisition-of-portkey-to-secure-ai-agents> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S012: Palo Alto Networks Inc - Form 10-K - FY2026 (fiscal year ended 31 July 2026) (US SEC EDGAR / Palo Alto Networks) <https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001327567/000132756726000023/panw-20260731.htm> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S025: Palo Alto Networks Completes Acquisition of Portkey to Secure AI Agents (with PANW FY2026 10-K and 10-Q, SDxCentral) <https://www.paloaltonetworks.com/company/press/2026/palo-alto-networks-completes-acquisition-of-portkey-to-secure-ai-agents> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Stripe agreed to acquire OpenRouter, with closing pending [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — V1-S059: Stripe agrees to acquire OpenRouter to help businesses optimize token routing and usage <https://stripe.com/newsroom/news/stripe-agrees-to-acquire-openrouter> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; V1-S060: Stripe to buy OpenRouter as fintech expands deeper into AI (and TechCrunch 16 Aug 2026 report) (CNBC / TechCrunch) <https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/19/stripe-openrouter-fintech-ai-model-marketplace-.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. A gateway owned by a model vendor, a payments company or a security vendor is not neutral, and that matters most for the component meant to keep you neutral [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The regulatory hooks.** From 18 March 2027, PRA PS7/26 and FCA PS26/2 require notification before entering a material third-party arrangement, so "add a provider" belongs with third-party risk [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); R-FCA-SYSC8: FCA Handbook SYSC 8 (Outsourcing); FG16/5 'Guidance for firms outsourcing to the cloud and other third-party IT services'; PS26/2 operational incident and third-party reporting (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S062: PS7/26 Operational incident and third-party reporting (PRA) and FCA PS26/2; revised SS2/21 (March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA; FCA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/prudential-regulation/publication/2026/march/operational-incident-and-third-party-reporting-policy-statement> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Where a use is high-risk under the EU AI Act, Article 26 requires deployers to keep logs for at least six months [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-EUAIA: Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (Artificial Intelligence Act), as amended by Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (Digital Omnibus on AI) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S011: Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (Digital Omnibus on AI) amending Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (EUR-Lex / Official Journal of the EU) <https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2026/1744/oj/eng> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The gateway sees every request with its model version, so its log is the natural evidence base [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The people who rehearse exits rarely get noticed until the day an exit works. They should be noticed before.

### What good looks like

The signal is **time to switch provider**: how long it takes to move a production route to a pre-qualified alternative by configuration alone, with no code change [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Measure it in a scheduled exit drill. Pick a route, move it to the qualified fallback through the gateway, confirm that quality stays within the evaluation thresholds and that processing stays in region, then move it back. Keep the drill record as evidence [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

The review's target guidance is hours, not weeks, tested at least once a year [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Three companion measures make the number credible. Gateway coverage, the share of production model, tool and agent calls that pass through the gateway, should be 100% in scope; any direct provider key in an application is a defect [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Residency conformance should show zero requests processed outside the approved region [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. And budget enforcement should show zero breaches that were neither blocked nor alerted, including when the spend store is lost [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. These are a starting point, not a benchmark [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 4: gateway.** One gateway route, attribution-commentary-draft: the workflow calls the route with its own identity and never holds a provider key; residency, fallback, budgets and inline guards live on the route. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** Switching to the qualified fallback is a configuration change, drilled in hours. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** If both models are unavailable, the route fails closed and the analyst is told the draft is delayed. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** The gateway's request record for every call. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Gateway coverage: 100% of model calls. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"A single gateway is a single point of failure."** It is, and the answer is not to remove it [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Deploy at least two independent instances, per region or per criticality tier, sharing configuration from one source [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Decide in advance what an outage means. For regulated outputs, "no AI until restored" is a better degraded mode than a direct path that bypasses every control [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Then treat it like any other critical service: tested failover, synthetic probes per route, and an incident classification under DORA's ICT incident regime [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DORA: Regulation (EU) 2022/2554 Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S021: Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) pages and RoI reporting FAQ (ESMA / EBA / EIOPA) <https://www.esma.europa.eu/esmas-activities/digital-finance-and-innovation/digital-operational-resilience-act-dora> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Concentration improves control and worsens resilience; good engineering keeps the first and limits the second [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Our API-management platform can already do this."** Perhaps it can, and Microsoft makes the same argument about its own [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S053: AI gateway capabilities in Azure API Management (Microsoft Learn source, ms.date 29 May 2026) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs/main/articles/api-management/genai-gateway-capabilities.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Use it if it carries model, tool and agent traffic with the controls above [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Two cautions. Use generally available features only, not a preview tier without an SLA [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S020: Azure API Management AI gateway docs and Tech Community announcements (AI Gateway tier preview; Build 2026 updates) (Microsoft) <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/api-management/ai-gateway-overview> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S073: AI Gateway tier (preview) overview - Azure API Management (with Tech Community 'AI Gateway tier of API Management now in public preview') (Microsoft) <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/api-management/ai-gateway-overview> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. And remember that policies in a proprietary dialect are part of the exit cost, so document their intent in plain terms [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"An open-source gateway was compromised. Why trust one?"** The compromise was real [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S008: Security Update: Suspected Supply Chain Incident (and Security Townhall Updates) (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/blog/security-update-march-2026> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. But every gateway, open or closed, holds the keys, so the question is supply-chain discipline, not licence model [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Mirror packages internally, verify signatures at admission and pin versions [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A managed gateway moves that work to a vendor, along with the ownership question this chapter has already raised [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### Questions for your team

- How long would it take us to move our most important AI route to its fallback, and when did we last do it? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Are there provider keys anywhere outside the gateway's secret store? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- If the gateway lost its budget or policy store, would requests fail open or fail closed? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Does every fallback model sit in the same approved region, and has it passed the route's regression suite? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Who owns the gateway product we depend on, and has that changed in the last year? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Does adding a model provider trigger our third-party risk process, with lead time for UK notification? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### In one line

Neutrality is a design property: build it into the gateway, then rehearse the exit until it is routine [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.


## 5. Most enterprise "agents" should be workflows

*L3 Agent frameworks and orchestration, from Post 5 of the series.*

### The post

Most enterprise "agents" worth approving are not agents at all. They are deterministic workflows with one judgement step. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The trap is handing a task with a known sequence to an autonomous loop. Each run takes a slightly different path, so evaluation results stop transferring between runs and validation can no longer cover the space of behaviours. When something goes wrong, the behaviour turns out to be a property of that run, not of the design. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Every major framework now ships both modes, a workflow graph and an agent loop, so this is a design choice rather than a vendor choice. Make it before choosing a framework. If the steps are known in advance, even with branches, build a graph. Let the model draft, classify or judge inside a bounded step; do not let it choose the next tool. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S039: LangGraph Persistence / Durable execution / Interrupts - Docs by LangChain <https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/durable-execution> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S066: Microsoft Agent Framework README (GitHub) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/agent-framework/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S050: CrewAI Introduction / Flows / Evaluating Use Cases - CrewAI docs <https://docs.crewai.com/en/introduction> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S114: Agent Development Kit (ADK) 2.0 README (GitHub) (Google) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/adk-python/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Two things sit underneath. Durable execution, so a crash resumes from a checkpoint instead of re-running and mixing two versions of the data. And an approval step that only a named, authorised person can release. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The signal is path conformance: the share of runs whose executed steps match the approved graph. For a regulated workflow the target is 100%, and any deviation is a defect. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The leadership move is to treat autonomy as a budget that is declared, approved and owned for each use case. Often the right number is zero or one. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The honest caveat: autonomous harnesses are moving fastest, and some open-ended tasks genuinely need them. Isolate them as one sandboxed sub-step with read-only tools rather than banning them. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S053: The next evolution of the Agents SDK (OpenAI) <https://openai.com/index/the-next-evolution-of-the-agents-sdk/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S027: Agent SDK overview - Claude Code Docs (Anthropic) <https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S057: Build AI agents with the Mistral Agents API (and Agents & Conversations / Handoffs docs) (Mistral AI) <https://mistral.ai/news/agents-api/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Autonomy is a tool, not a maturity level. The best agent design is often the least agentic one that does the job. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

![Most enterprise "agents" should be workflows](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P05.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 5. The worked example's commentary graph: a pinned sequence of steps with one bounded drafting step, a hard evaluation gate and a human approval interrupt, on a durable checkpoint store. Generic: no vendor names.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

This layer decides how an AI application sequences model calls, tool calls, state and human decisions, and what happens when a step fails half-way [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Most of that is plumbing. One part of it is not: the decision, for each use case, about how much of the control flow the model is given [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. That decision shapes validation, audit and incident response more than any choice of framework does.

**Two kinds of orchestration.** The useful line runs between workflows, where models and tools are orchestrated through predefined code paths, and agents, where the model dynamically directs its own process and tool use. Anthropic's published guidance draws the line in those terms [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S030: Building effective agents (Anthropic) <https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/building-effective-agents> accessed 2026-10-07]. It is not one vendor's view: Microsoft Agent Framework separates Agents from Workflows, and CrewAI separates Crews from Flows and recommends Flows as the outer process [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S066: Microsoft Agent Framework README (GitHub) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/agent-framework/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S050: CrewAI Introduction / Flows / Evaluating Use Cases - CrewAI docs <https://docs.crewai.com/en/introduction> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].

**Why autonomy is expensive in a regulated firm.** When the model chooses the next step, each run can take a different path. A test suite that passed last week tells you about last week's paths, not tomorrow's [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The validated object should therefore be the workflow version (the graph, prompts, model versions and tool contracts) rather than the model alone, and a change to any of them is a change requiring re-evaluation [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The deterministic parts are not exempt because they are code. PRA SS1/23 says relevant model risk management aspects can be applied to material, complex deterministic quantitative methods that are not models [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS123: PRA Supervisory Statement SS1/23 'Model risk management principles for banks' (published with PS6/23) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S008: SS1/23 Model risk management principles for banks (Bank of England / PRA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/prudential-regulation/publication/2023/may/model-risk-management-principles-for-banks-ss> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. In the US, SR 26-2 superseded SR 11-7 on 17 April 2026 and places generative and agentic AI expressly outside its scope, which leaves the firm to set its own standard [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-US-MRM: Revised Guidance on Model Risk Management: Federal Reserve SR 26-2 / OCC Bulletin 2026-13 / FDIC FIL-15-2026 (supersedes SR 11-7 and SR 21-8) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S001: SR 26-2: Revised Guidance on Model Risk Management (17 April 2026) (Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System) <https://www.federalreserve.gov/supervisionreg/srletters/SR2602.htm> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A8-S002: OCC Bulletin 2026-13 Model Risk Management: Revised Guidance (attachment bulletin-2026-13a.pdf) (Office of the Comptroller of the Currency) <https://www.occ.gov/news-issuances/bulletins/2026/bulletin-2026-13.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Deterministic graphs make that standard testable [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**What changed in 2025–26.** Four shifts, all pointing the same way.

- *Both modes in every major framework.* LangGraph combines deterministic logic and model-driven decisions in one graph; Microsoft Agent Framework reached general availability on 2 April 2026 as the successor to Semantic Kernel and AutoGen, with AutoGen in maintenance mode; Google ADK 2.0 added a Workflow Runtime [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S039: LangGraph Persistence / Durable execution / Interrupts - Docs by LangChain <https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/durable-execution> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S008: PyPI JSON metadata: agent-framework (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/agent-framework/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S012: PyPI JSON metadata: semantic-kernel (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/semantic-kernel/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S021: PyPI JSON metadata: autogen-agentchat (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/autogen-agentchat/json> accessed 2026-10-07; V1-S050: PyPI JSON metadata: agent-framework (1.0.0 uploaded 2026-04-02; latest 1.20.0) (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/agent-framework/json> accessed 2026-10-08; A4-S114: Agent Development Kit (ADK) 2.0 README (GitHub) (Google) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/adk-python/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. The choice between a graph and a loop is now made inside one framework, not between vendors [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.
- *Durability separated out.* Pydantic AI v2 attaches durability through Temporal, DBOS or Prefect; the OpenAI Agents SDK integrates Temporal and DBOS; Mistral Workflows is built on Temporal [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S045: Pydantic AI Durable Execution (overview, Temporal, DBOS, Prefect) <https://pydantic.dev/docs/ai/capabilities/durable_execution/overview/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S052: OpenAI Agents SDK documentation (agents, sessions, handoffs, guardrails, tracing, running agents) <https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S058: Workflows for work that runs the business (Mistral Workflows) (Mistral AI) <https://mistral.ai/news/workflows/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].
- *Runtimes separated from frameworks.* Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runs agents written with several frameworks and gives each session its own microVM [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S116: Bedrock AgentCore SDK README (GitHub) (AWS) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aws/bedrock-agentcore-sdk-python/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; B-L3-S004: AgentCore Runtime sessions / microVMs / quotas / security best practices (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/runtime-sessions.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Its session state is ephemeral by default, and AWS says it should not be used for long-term durability [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L3-S004: AgentCore Runtime sessions / microVMs / quotas / security best practices (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/runtime-sessions.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. A managed runtime is an isolation choice, not a substitute for durable workflow state [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.
- *Visual builders retreating.* OpenAI's Agent Builder shuts down on 30 November 2026, and OpenAI points code users to its SDK [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S054: OpenAI Deprecations (Agent Builder) and Introducing AgentKit update <https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/deprecations> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V1-S051: OpenAI API Deprecations (Agent Builder row); Introducing AgentKit (updated) <https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/deprecations> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]].

**Durable execution, in plain terms.** A durable engine records the result of each side-effecting step, so that after a failure the workflow is replayed and completed steps return their recorded results instead of running again [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S019: PyPI JSON metadata: temporalio (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/temporalio/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S045: Pydantic AI Durable Execution (overview, Temporal, DBOS, Prefect) <https://pydantic.dev/docs/ai/capabilities/durable_execution/overview/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. For a commentary workflow, that means a crash after the attribution snapshot was fetched resumes with the same snapshot. The draft and its evidence refer to the same numbers [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Without it, a retry can silently pick up a late price correction and produce a document nobody can trace to its data [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The checkpoint store holds that evidence, so it is also a security boundary: LangGraph has published three checkpoint deserialisation advisories since 2025, all patched [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L3-S007: LangGraph security advisories (GitHub) and NVD entries (LangChain / GitHub / NIST NVD) <https://github.com/langchain-ai/langgraph/security> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]].

**Approval as a control, not a convention.** A "human review" step that code can skip, or that does not record who approved, is not a gate [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. LangGraph interrupts, Microsoft Agent Framework human-in-the-loop, ADK tool confirmation and the OpenAI Agents SDK's resumable approvals all support an approval that pauses the run until a person resumes it [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S118: LangGraph README (GitHub) (LangChain) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langchain-ai/langgraph/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S066: Microsoft Agent Framework README (GitHub) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/agent-framework/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S114: Agent Development Kit (ADK) 2.0 README (GitHub) (Google) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/adk-python/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S120: OpenAI Agents SDK README (GitHub) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/openai-agents-python/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Supervisors are watching this point. ESMA holds management bodies responsible for decisions taken by AI tools, and the Bank of England reported that 55% of AI use cases had some autonomous decision-making and 2% were fully autonomous [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-INTL-AI-ASSETMGMT: IOSCO Supervisory Toolkit for AI Use in Capital Markets (FR/02/2026) and ESMA Public Statement on AI and investment services (30 May 2024) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S059: ESMA Public Statement on AI and investment services (30 May 2024) and 2026 briefings <https://www.esma.europa.eu/document/public-statement-ai-and-investment-services> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; R-UK-AI-STATEMENTS: FCA, Bank of England and PRA statements and initiatives on AI, 2024-2026 (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S056: Financial Policy Committee Record April 2026; FSR July 2026; 2026 AI survey (Bank of England) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/financial-policy-committee-record/2026/april-2026> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].

**The worked example.** The review's generic example is an agent that drafts monthly performance-attribution commentary for a multi-asset fund. Its graph is pinned: authorise the request, fetch the attribution snapshot through a read-only tool, retrieve house style and prior commentary, run one model drafting step, pass an evaluation gate, stop for a human approval interrupt, then hand to a separate release service that acts on the approver's identity [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The model drafts and explains. It never picks the next tool, and a failed numeric check routes back for revision, never to approval [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. That is an autonomy budget of one.

**Where autonomous harnesses fit.** They are useful for open-ended, file- or code-centric sub-tasks [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The review assessed two from model vendors. The OpenAI Agents SDK is pre-1.0 and its tracing defaults to OpenAI's dashboard; the Claude Agent SDK carries an Alpha classifier and keeps session transcripts on local disk unless a session store is configured [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S005: PyPI JSON metadata: openai-agents (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/openai-agents/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S052: OpenAI Agents SDK documentation (agents, sessions, handoffs, guardrails, tracing, running agents) <https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S006: PyPI JSON metadata: claude-agent-sdk (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/claude-agent-sdk/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S122: Hosting the Agent SDK - Claude Code Docs (Anthropic) <https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/hosting> accessed 2026-10-07]. At the review's fourth checkpoint both were given the same maturity score, and they were rated Tactical and Experimental respectively [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A disclosure: this book was prepared with the help of an Anthropic model, and the review resolved borderline calls on the Anthropic product against it [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Either harness belongs inside a firm-owned workflow as one replaceable, sandboxed node, with a model-agnostic equivalent on LangGraph or Pydantic AI kept available [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

The credit for this design rarely goes where it should. It belongs to the engineers who make the boring path reliable [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### What good looks like

The signal is **path conformance**: the share of runs whose executed node sequence matches the approved graph version [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Measure it by comparing each run's trace with the graph version recorded at the start of the run, which requires every node to emit a span and every run to carry its graph version [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. For a regulated workflow the starting target is 100%, and any deviation is treated as a defect to be investigated, not a statistic to be averaged [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Two companion measures make the number meaningful. *Resume without re-execution*: in fault-injection tests, every interrupted run resumes from its last checkpoint without repeating a completed side-effecting step. *Approval-gate integrity*: every release carries a recorded approval by an authorised person, with zero bypasses, reconciled against publish events [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Track the declared autonomy budget per use case alongside them: steps where the model chooses the next action, and the maximum tool calls per run [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

These targets are a starting point drawn from the review's guidance, not an industry benchmark [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Where a use case genuinely needs an autonomous sub-step, path conformance applies to the outer graph, and the sub-step is measured on its own allow-list and trajectory checks [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 5: workflow.** A pinned workflow graph: authorise, fetch the attribution snapshot, retrieve style and prior commentary, one drafting step, an evaluation gate, a human approval interrupt, then release by a separate service. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** Every run follows the same path; a failed run resumes from its checkpoint with the same numbers. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** Autonomy budget of one: the model drafts, but never chooses tools or order. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** The run's path through the graph and its checkpoints. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Path conformance: 100% of runs on the approved path. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"This throws away what makes agents valuable."** It does not ban them; it admits them where they earn their place [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Because every major framework now ships both modes, moving a step from a graph into a bounded agent loop later is a design change inside one framework, not a migration [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S039: LangGraph Persistence / Durable execution / Interrupts - Docs by LangChain <https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/durable-execution> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A4-S066: Microsoft Agent Framework README (GitHub) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/agent-framework/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. What the approach refuses is autonomy by default for a process whose sequence is already known [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The value of a model in most regulated processes lies in drafting, classifying and explaining, all of which fit inside a bounded step [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Real processes have exceptions; a graph will be brittle."** Graphs can branch, loop and route exceptions to a person [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A4-S114: Agent Development Kit (ADK) 2.0 README (GitHub) (Google) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/adk-python/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The test is whether the steps are known in advance, even with branches, not whether the process is simple [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A bounded retry edge back to the drafting step, with a limit, handles most of what teams expect autonomy to solve [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Where the exceptions really are open-ended, that is the signal to carve out one sandboxed agent step, not to make the whole process autonomous [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Durable execution is heavy infrastructure for one use case."** It can be. Self-hosting Temporal means operating Kubernetes and persistence stores and upgrading in sequence [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L3-S002: Temporal server repository; production deployment and Cloud overview docs (Temporal Technologies Inc.) <https://github.com/temporalio/temporal> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. But Temporal Cloud runs the same server, DBOS describes itself as ultra-lightweight, and a LangGraph-only estate can use its own checkpointer [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L3-S002: Temporal server repository; production deployment and Cloud overview docs (Temporal Technologies Inc.) <https://github.com/temporalio/temporal> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; A4-S020: PyPI JSON metadata: dbos (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/dbos/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A4-S039: LangGraph Persistence / Durable execution / Interrupts - Docs by LangChain <https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/durable-execution> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The alternative cost is a run that cannot be reconstructed after a crash, which is harder to explain to an auditor than a database is to operate [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### Questions for your team

- For each use case in flight, is the sequence of steps known in advance? If so, why is it built as a loop? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- What is the declared autonomy budget for each workflow, and who approved it? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- If a run crashes after fetching data, does it resume with the same data, and can we prove it in a kill-and-resume test? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Can our code skip the human approval step, and does the record show who approved what? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Do we record the graph version on every run, and measure path conformance against it? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Where an autonomous harness is in use, is it sandboxed, read-only, and replaceable by a model-agnostic equivalent? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### In one line

Decide how much autonomy a process deserves before choosing a framework, and the answer for most regulated work will be zero or one. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}


## 6. Guardrails cannot fix the wrong autonomy

*C2 Guardrails, from Post 6 of the series.*

### The post

Guardrails cannot fix a workflow that should never have been autonomous. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

A guardrail is a filter on a stream of actions. It lowers the chance that a bad action passes; it does not reduce the number of actions an agent is allowed to attempt. If an agent has write access to a client-facing system, a content filter on its output is not a control over that access. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Deterministic workflows with one judgement step do most of the safety work. Guardrails are the second line, and they fail in predictable ways. They screen only the user's prompt while retrieved documents pass unchecked. A content-safety classifier is asked to catch a business error it was never trained for, such as a reversed sign. Or a guard times out and the request goes through. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

So layer them by mechanism. Deterministic rules first, for business invariants such as figures, signs and forbidden phrases. Small classifiers next, including on every retrieved chunk. Model-based judges only where nothing simpler can decide, and never as the only check on numbers. Everything fails closed. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The signal worth watching is the false-positive rate on a labelled set of real, approved finance text, for example below 1% on a drafting route. Over-sensitive filters block legitimate vocabulary, people route around them, and the control quietly disappears. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The leadership move is to own the guardrail policy and its test sets, whichever detectors you buy. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The honest caveat: several open-source guardrail components have slowed or changed hands. Using two detectors from different owners is a concentration control as well as a security one. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S003: nemoguardrails - PyPI JSON metadata (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/nemoguardrails/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S107: Llama Guard 4 model card; Llama Prompt Guard 2 model card; Llama Protections (Meta) <https://developer.meta.com/ai/docs/model-cards-and-prompt-formats/llama-guard-4/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S028: Guardrails AI Joins Harvey <https://www.harvey.ai/blog/guardrails-ai-joins-harvey> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

A guardrail is evidence of care. It is not a substitute for deciding what the system may do. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

![Guardrails cannot fix the wrong autonomy](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P06.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 6. Decide autonomy first; then layer guardrails by mechanism, with cheap deterministic checks first at the base and everything failing closed. The 1% false-positive figure is an example target from the review, not a benchmark.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

Guardrails are the run-time checks on either side of a model or agent call. They inspect what goes in (user input, retrieved documents, tool results) and what comes out (text, tool calls), and they block, transform or flag it against policy [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. They are distinct from evaluation and red-teaming, which test a system before release but block nothing at run time [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. They are also distinct from two decisions they are often asked to stand in for: how much autonomy a workflow has, and whether a given tool call is authorised [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The mechanism, and its limit.** A guardrail reduces the probability that a bad action passes. It does not reduce the number of actions an agent may attempt [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. That matters most for the first entry on the OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications for 2026, Agent Goal Hijack [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-OWASP-AGENTIC: OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications for 2026 (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S042: OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications for 2026 (launched 9-10 December 2025) (OWASP GenAI Security Project) <https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-top-10-for-agentic-applications-for-2026/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The most effective mitigation for goal hijack is to give the agent no goal it can be hijacked towards: fixed steps, read-only tools and a scoped identity [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. In the review's worked example, a deterministic workflow drafting monthly attribution commentary, most of the safety comes from that design, and the guardrails are the second line [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Four predictable failures.** Badly designed guardrails go wrong in the *wrong place*, screening only the user's prompt while retrieved documents and tool results, the real injection path for agents, pass unchecked. They use the *wrong mechanism*, asking a content-safety classifier to catch a business error such as a wrong sign on a currency effect. They generate *false positives that drive bypass*. And they *fail open* when a guard times out [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Three mechanisms, layered.** The distinction matters for latency, cost, explainability and false positives [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

- *Deterministic rules*: word filters, denied topics, entity patterns and numeric comparators. Fast and explainable, with false positives that are predictable and fixable [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.
- *Small classifiers.* Prompt Guard 2's 22M-parameter variant is designed for low-latency, low-compute use, while Llama Guard 4 is a 12B model needing GPU-class serving [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S030: meta-llama/Llama-Guard-4-12B model card and LICENSE (Hugging Face); Llama Prompt Guard 2 model cards (Meta (via Hugging Face)) <https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-Guard-4-12B> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Prompt Guard 2 has a 512-token window, so long documents must be chunked before screening [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S030: meta-llama/Llama-Guard-4-12B model card and LICENSE (Hugging Face); Llama Prompt Guard 2 model cards (Meta (via Hugging Face)) <https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-Guard-4-12B> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.
- *Model-based judges*: an LLM checking the output. Flexible but slow and costly, and they can be manipulated by the same text they judge [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Route design follows: rules everywhere, small classifiers on all untrusted input, and judges only where nothing cheaper can decide [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Unit costs differ by mechanism as well. As of 7 October 2026, Amazon Bedrock Guardrails billed content filters and denied topics at US$0.15 per 1,000 text units per policy, while Google's Model Armor was free to 2M tokens a month and then US$0.10 per 1M tokens [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S101: Amazon Bedrock Pricing (Guardrails); Guardrails reduces pricing by up to 85% (Dec 2024); Safeguard tiers (June 2025) (Amazon Web Services) <https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S067: Model Armor product page (incl. pricing) (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/model-armor> accessed 2026-10-07].

**Failing closed is a design choice.** NVIDIA NeMo Guardrails' IORails engine distinguishes policy blocks from rail execution failures [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S036: NeMo Guardrails CHANGELOG.md (NVIDIA) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Guardrails/develop/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07], which is what lets a route fail closed on errors without treating every error as a policy event [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Defaults vary: LiteLLM's prompt-injection guardrails are off by default [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S015: LiteLLM docs: MCP Overview, Agent Gateway (A2A), Caching, Budgets/Rate Limits, v1.89.0 release notes (BerriAI (LiteLLM)) <https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/mcp> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Configure and test the behaviour explicitly [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**What changed in 2025–26.** The hyperscalers now ship broad managed services. Bedrock Guardrails covers content and prompt-attack filters, denied topics, PII, contextual grounding and Automated Reasoning checks [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S072: AWS API model: Amazon Bedrock control plane (botocore service-2.json) - guardrail shapes (Amazon Web Services (via boto/botocore)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/boto/botocore/develop/botocore/data/bedrock/2023-04-20/service-2.json> accessed 2026-10-07]. Azure Prompt Shields has been generally available since August 2024, with Task Adherence for agent tool use in preview [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S055: What's new in Azure AI Content Safety? (Microsoft Learn source) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MicrosoftDocs/azure-ai-docs/main/articles/ai-services/content-safety/whats-new.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Model Armor enforces data residency by default [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S067: Model Armor product page (incl. pricing) (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/model-armor> accessed 2026-10-07; B-C2-S003: Model Armor: Data residency and endpoints (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/model-armor/data-residency> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. The review rates each as Strategic only where that cloud is the firm's primary cloud [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

The open-source side is more fragile. NeMo Guardrails is still 0.x Beta [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S003: nemoguardrails - PyPI JSON metadata (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/nemoguardrails/json> accessed 2026-10-07]. Meta has released no new Llama Guard, Prompt Guard or LlamaFirewall since May 2025 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S006: llamafirewall - PyPI JSON metadata (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/llamafirewall/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S107: Llama Guard 4 model card; Llama Prompt Guard 2 model card; Llama Protections (Meta) <https://developer.meta.com/ai/docs/model-cards-and-prompt-formats/llama-guard-4/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].
