![Retrieval quality is a two-stage problem](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P13.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 13. Measure each stage on its own number, and change embedding models by dual index behind a regression gate. Targets are starting points, not benchmarks; version labels are illustrative.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

**Two models doing two different jobs.** An embedding model encodes the query and each document separately, so documents can be encoded in advance. That is fast, and it is coarse [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A reranker reads the query and each candidate together. That is slower and more precise, which is why it is applied only to a short candidate list [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Current rerankers take long inputs: Cohere Rerank 4 handles 32K tokens and JSON documents, Voyage rerank-3 has a 32K-token context, and Jina reranker v3.5 scores candidates jointly rather than one at a time [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S009: Rerank 4: Cohere's Most Powerful Reranker Yet <https://cohere.com/blog/rerank-4> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S034: rerank-3 and rerank-3-lite: the next generation of Voyage rerankers (Voyage AI (MongoDB)) <https://blog.voyageai.com/2026/09/30/rerank-3/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S026: jina-reranker-v3.5: Faster Listwise Reranking with Hybrid Attention and Self-Distillation (Jina AI (Elastic)) <https://jina.ai/en-US/news/jina-reranker-v3-5-faster-listwise-reranking-hybrid-attention-self-distillation/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The first stage also increasingly runs lexical search beside the dense one. Exact identifiers such as fund codes, ISINs and share-class names are what dense vectors blur and lexical matching catches [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Fusing the two lists is now a feature of the store itself, in Qdrant, Elasticsearch, MongoDB, Pinecone and pgvector [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S103: Qdrant 1.17 - Relevance Feedback & Search Latency Improvements <https://qdrant.tech/blog/qdrant-1.17.x/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S133: Elastic 9.5: Columnar, VectorDB index mode & auto-calibration, and AI-driven alert triage <https://www.elastic.co/blog/whats-new-elastic-9-5-0> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S141: Context Engineering Has a Retrieval Issue: Introducing rerank-3 and Native Reranking (MongoDB) <https://www.mongodb.com/company/blog/product-release-announcements/introducing-rerank-3-and-native-rerank> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S101: Introducing cascading retrieval: Unifying dense and sparse with reranking (Pinecone) <https://www.pinecone.io/blog/cascading-retrieval/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S063: pgvector CHANGELOG.md (pgvector project) <https://github.com/pgvector/pgvector/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]].

The failure the post describes follows from that division of labour. If the right passage never reaches the candidate list, no reranker can promote it. If it is there but ranked fortieth, a new embedding model is an expensive way to fix an ordering problem [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Measuring each stage on its own number is what tells a team which one to work on.

**What changed in 2025–26.** Every model vendor in this layer except OpenAI now offers both an embedding model and a reranker. Google's reranker is a separate Vertex ranking API [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S012: Announcing Cohere's Embed 5 Models <https://docs.cohere.com/changelog/embed-v5> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S010: Cohere's Rerank v4.0 Model is Here! <https://docs.cohere.com/changelog/rerank-v4.0> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S006: The Voyage 4 model family: shared embedding space with MoE architecture (Voyage AI (MongoDB)) <https://blog.voyageai.com/2026/01/15/voyage-4/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S034: rerank-3 and rerank-3-lite: the next generation of Voyage rerankers (Voyage AI (MongoDB)) <https://blog.voyageai.com/2026/09/30/rerank-3/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S025: jina-embeddings-v5-omni: Embeddings for Text, Image, Audio and Video (Jina AI (Elastic)) <https://jina.ai/news/jina-embeddings-v5-omni-multimodal-embeddings-for-text-image-audio-and-video/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S026: jina-reranker-v3.5: Faster Listwise Reranking with Hybrid Attention and Self-Distillation (Jina AI (Elastic)) <https://jina.ai/en-US/news/jina-reranker-v3-5-faster-listwise-reranking-hybrid-attention-self-distillation/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S030: Release Notes for NVIDIA NeMo Retriever Reranking NIM <https://docs.nvidia.com/nim/nemo-retriever/text-reranking/latest/release-notes.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S031: Get Started With NVIDIA NeMo Retriever Embedding NIM (2.3) <https://docs.nvidia.com/nim/nemo-retriever/embedding/2.3/getting-started.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S020: Qwen3 Embedding: Advancing Text Embedding and Reranking Through Foundation Models (Alibaba Qwen) <https://qwen.ai/blog?id=qwen3-embedding> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; B-REV-S026: Improve search and RAG quality with ranking API (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/generative-ai-app-builder/docs/ranking> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Two familiar vendors now belong to database companies: Voyage AI to MongoDB since 17 February 2025, and Jina AI to Elastic since 9 October 2025 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S033: MongoDB Announces Acquisition of Voyage AI to Enable Organizations to Build Trustworthy AI Applications <https://investors.mongodb.com/news-releases/news-release-details/mongodb-announces-acquisition-voyage-ai-enable-organizations> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V1-S023: MongoDB, Inc. Form 10-K FY2026 (Voyage AI acquisition note) and IR release 24 Feb 2025 (MongoDB, Inc. (SEC EDGAR)) <https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1441816/000162828026016799/mdb-20260131.htm> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; A2-S023: Elastic Completes Acquisition of Jina AI, a Leader in Frontier Models for Multimodal and Multilingual Search <https://ir.elastic.co/News--Events/news/news-details/2025/Elastic-Completes-Acquisition-of-Jina-AI-a-Leader-in-Frontier-Models-for-Multimodal-and-Multilingual-Search/default.aspx> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V1-S025: Elastic Completes Acquisition of Jina AI (Elastic N.V.) <https://ir.elastic.co/News--Events/news/news-details/2025/Elastic-Completes-Acquisition-of-Jina-AI-a-Leader-in-Frontier-Models-for-Multimodal-and-Multilingual-Search/default.aspx> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. And the stores now host the models. MongoDB runs Voyage cross-encoders in an aggregation stage (in preview), Pinecone hosts rerankers, and Elastic's `semantic_text` field embeds automatically with Jina v5 by default [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S141: Context Engineering Has a Retrieval Issue: Introducing rerank-3 and Native Reranking (MongoDB) <https://www.mongodb.com/company/blog/product-release-announcements/introducing-rerank-3-and-native-rerank> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S101: Introducing cascading retrieval: Unifying dense and sparse with reranking (Pinecone) <https://www.pinecone.io/blog/cascading-retrieval/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S133: Elastic 9.5: Columnar, VectorDB index mode & auto-calibration, and AI-driven alert triage <https://www.elastic.co/blog/whats-new-elastic-9-5-0> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The review's conclusion is that embeddings and reranking form one retrieval-optimisation layer, defined by responsibility rather than by where the compute runs [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Someone must still own the model choice, the version pin and the evaluation, even when the store does the work [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Why the version is production configuration.** Vectors from one embedding model cannot be compared with vectors from another [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Changing the model means re-embedding the whole corpus, and mixing two versions in one index degrades quality without raising an error [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The review's illustrative scenario makes the point. A team upgrades its embedding model on the ingestion and query paths but does not re-embed the existing chunks. New documents land in a new vector space while old ones stay in the old. Over the next fortnight the assistant starts missing the house style guide. Because nothing recorded which model produced which vectors, the only remedy is a full rebuild [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The controls are equally plain. Put a model-version tag on every vector, use a query path that refuses to mix versions, and run a retrieval regression suite on every configuration change [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Chapter 14 extends the same discipline to prompts.

Migration then becomes routine. Build the new index in shadow from stored raw text, compare both on the golden set and on shadow traffic, switch the read alias, and keep the old index until the rollback window closes [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Dimension and precision belong in the same record. Several current models let a team truncate vectors and lower precision with a controlled loss, and Elasticsearch has quantised by default since 9.1 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S005: Embeddings (Gemini API docs) / Gemini Embedding 2 preview model (Google) <https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/embeddings> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S012: Announcing Cohere's Embed 5 Models <https://docs.cohere.com/changelog/embed-v5> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S007: Voyage AI Embedding and Reranking Models Overview - Voyage AI by MongoDB <https://www.mongodb.com/docs/voyageai/models/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S133: Elastic 9.5: Columnar, VectorDB index mode & auto-calibration, and AI-driven alert triage <https://www.elastic.co/blog/whats-new-elastic-9-5-0> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Those choices change the index, so they are recorded with the model version [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Shared embedding spaces, in which several model sizes from one family sit in one space, reduce re-embedding inside a family. They do nothing for a move between vendors [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S006: The Voyage 4 model family: shared embedding space with MoE architecture (Voyage AI (MongoDB)) <https://blog.voyageai.com/2026/01/15/voyage-4/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S012: Announcing Cohere's Embed 5 Models <https://docs.cohere.com/changelog/embed-v5> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S025: jina-embeddings-v5-omni: Embeddings for Text, Image, Audio and Video (Jina AI (Elastic)) <https://jina.ai/news/jina-embeddings-v5-omni-multimodal-embeddings-for-text-image-audio-and-video/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**What re-embedding actually costs.** The API bill is the small part. A corpus of 2 million chunks at about 500 tokens each is roughly a billion tokens. At list prices as of 7 October 2026, that is about US$130 with OpenAI text-embedding-3-large, about US$60 with voyage-4, or about US$120 with Cohere Embed 5 Pro [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S001: text-embedding-3-large Model (OpenAI) <https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/text-embedding-3-large> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S007: Voyage AI Embedding and Reranking Models Overview - Voyage AI by MongoDB <https://www.mongodb.com/docs/voyageai/models/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S013: Embed 5: Frontier Embedding Models for Enterprise (Cohere) <https://cohere.com/blog/embed-5> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. The arithmetic is the review's own [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The real costs are a second index running in parallel, a fresh retrieval and answer-quality evaluation, revalidation evidence and the migration window [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. That is why the model version belongs in the exit plan as well as in configuration.

**Choosing by your own evaluation.** The review could not fetch the live public leaderboard, so it asserts no current ranking [NPV]{custom-style="Claim Label"}, and it treats vendor scores as reported claims rather than decision inputs [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The recommended method is an in-domain bake-off on 100 to 300 labelled queries written by the analysts who will use the system [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Compare a general model, a finance-domain model such as voyage-finance-2, and an open model fine-tuned with Sentence Transformers on house vocabulary [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S007: Voyage AI Embedding and Reranking Models Overview - Voyage AI by MongoDB <https://www.mongodb.com/docs/voyageai/models/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S029: sentence-transformers - PyPI (PyPI / Hugging Face (maintainer)) <https://pypi.org/project/sentence-transformers/> accessed 2026-10-07]. Pick by first-stage recall and ranking quality, not by vendor claims [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The regulated angle.** A change to the embedding model or reranker changes what the generative system sees. It should be treated as a material change that triggers the retrieval regression and sign-off [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Residency deserves a specific check: OpenAI's UK region is storage-only for embeddings, and Gemini's EU endpoint excludes the UK [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S144: Data controls in the OpenAI platform <https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/your-data> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A2-S039: Data residency (Vertex AI generative AI) (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/learn/data-residency> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. No hosted API in the review verified UK processing, which points UK-only data to in-estate or UK-region deployment [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Choosing MongoDB with Voyage, or Elastic with Jina, also concentrates two layers on one supplier. That can be accepted, but consciously and in the exit plan [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"} [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### What good looks like

The first-stage signal is **recall@k**: the share of golden-set questions whose relevant passage appears in the top k candidates, with k between 50 and 100 [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Measure it offline on a labelled, in-domain question set on every model or configuration change. The starting target is 0.95 [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

The second-stage signal is ranking quality after the reranker, measured as nDCG@10 or precision@5 on the same set with graded relevance labels. The starting rule is no regression of more than two points on release [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Two more numbers keep the layer governable. **Version consistency**, the share of vectors in a live index carrying the index's declared model version, should be 100% [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. **Full re-embed time** into a shadow index should sit inside the agreed change window, tested twice a year [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

All of these are starting points for a firm to calibrate on its own corpus. They are not industry benchmarks [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Where a corpus spans languages, add a parity check, with a starting gap of no more than five points between English and other in-scope languages [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 13: retrieval quality.** Two-stage retrieval of comparable past commentary (embed and rerank), with both model versions pinned and tagged on every vector. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** The draft follows the fund's own style and comparable months. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** A mixed-version index is refused; migration runs as a shadow index behind a regression gate. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** Embedding and reranker versions per run. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Stage-one recall@k on a labelled question set. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"Just pick the model at the top of the leaderboard."** Public benchmarks are a reasonable shortlist, but a firm's corpus is not the benchmark's corpus [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Fund codes, house terminology and the way a team phrases attribution are exactly where general models differ. A few hundred labelled questions, written by the people who will use the system, settle it on the firm's own ground [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The same set then becomes the regression gate for every later change, so the effort is not spent once [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Running two indexes doubles the storage bill."** For a while, roughly, yes. The alternative is an unplanned re-embed under time pressure, with no clean way back and no comparison to show a validator [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The overlap is also bounded: the old index is retired as soon as the rollback window closes [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Quantisation and reduced dimensions can shrink the shadow index if storage is tight. Cohere's own worked example shows a 100M-chunk index falling from about 819 GB to 3.2 GB [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A2-S013: Embed 5: Frontier Embedding Models for Enterprise (Cohere) <https://cohere.com/blog/embed-5> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"A reranker adds latency the users will feel."** It does, which is why the review caps the candidate count, typically 25 to 100, and measures the recall it buys [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A starting latency budget is under 300 ms at p95 for 50 candidates [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A reranker is also the cheapest component to change: it is stateless, and swapping it needs re-evaluation but no re-index [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. If the latency still hurts, the honest test is whether ranking quality without it stays above threshold on the golden set. If it does, the reranker was not earning its place [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### Questions for your team

- Do we know our first-stage recall and our post-rerank ranking quality as two separate numbers?
- Who wrote our golden question set, and does it reflect how analysts actually ask?
- Does every vector carry the model version that produced it, and can a query mix versions?
- Which embedding and reranker versions are pinned, and where is that pin recorded?
- How long would a full re-embed into a shadow index take today?
- Where are our embedding calls processed, as distinct from where the vectors are stored?
- Does our store embed content with a default model nobody chose? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### In one line

Measure each retrieval stage on its own number, and treat the embedding version as production configuration that changes only behind a gate [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.


## 14. A prompt change is a production change

*C5 Prompt and configuration management, from Post 14 of the series.*

### The post

A one-sentence edit to a prompt can change outputs as much as a model upgrade. Few organisations would let a model upgrade ship without review. Many let prompts change in a web console. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Prompt registries are sold on exactly that convenience: update the text, no deployment needed. That is useful while iterating. On a regulated output, it means one person can write, approve and release a change that nobody can later trace. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S071: Langfuse docs: Prompt Management overview (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse-docs/main/content/docs/prompt-management/overview.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The embedding model version is production configuration. So is the prompt. So are the model version, the retrieval settings, the tool list and the guardrail policy. Each can change what a client reads; each needs versioning, review, an evaluation gate and a way back. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The pattern worth defending is plain. Source control is the system of record. Every approved prompt and setting goes into one pinned release manifest, approved by pull request with a second reviewer and gated by the regression suite. A registry may deliver approved versions at run time and stamp each trace with the version that produced it, but only the release pipeline moves the production label. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The signal is rollback time: from the decision to revert to the previous approved version serving all traffic. Minutes, not a release cycle. Drill it twice a year. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The leadership move is to decide which configuration changes are material, and therefore trigger re-validation, before the first one happens.

The honest caveat: this adds friction to prompt iteration, and teams will feel it. The answer is a fast path for experiments on separate keys, not a weaker path to production. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

If a change can alter what a client reads, it deserves the same discipline as code.

![A prompt change is a production change](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P14.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 14. Every setting that can change what a client reads goes into one pinned manifest; only the release pipeline moves the production label. Manifest values are illustrative.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

**Prompts are code that anyone can edit.** A prompt is code that non-engineers can change, that no compiler can check, and whose effect shows only in the outputs [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The configuration that determines an output is also wider than the prompt [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. It includes the system prompt, templates and examples; the model, pinned to a dated version, with its parameters; the tool list and tool descriptions; the embedding model, reranker, chunking and top-k (Chapter 13); the primary and fallback routes; and the guardrail policy and evaluation thresholds [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Change any of these and what a client reads can change.

**The market sells the convenience.** Prompt management has become a capability inside other products rather than a category of its own [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Langfuse and LangSmith each ship a prompt registry inside their observability and evaluation platforms [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S071: Langfuse docs: Prompt Management overview (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse-docs/main/content/docs/prompt-management/overview.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A7-S076: LangSmith docs: Manage prompts (LangChain) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langchain-ai/docs/main/src/langsmith/manage-prompts.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07]. LaunchDarkly delivers model and prompt configuration through its feature-flag service, and renamed the product from AI Configs to AgentControl in 2026 with the API unchanged [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S117: LaunchDarkly: Introducing AgentControl; AI Configs is now GA; AgentControl API docs <https://launchdarkly.com/blog/introducing-agentcontrol/> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S045: AgentControl API docs (LaunchDarkly) <https://launchdarkly.com/docs/api/agentcontrol> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. PromptLayer remains an independent registry [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S004: promptlayer on PyPI (JSON API) (PyPI / PromptLayer) <https://pypi.org/pypi/promptlayer/json> accessed 2026-10-07]. Alongside them, the "prompts as code" pattern keeps prompt files in open formats such as Prompty and Dotprompt in the application repository and tests them in CI [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S067: Prompty README (Microsoft) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/prompty/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A7-S066: Dotprompt README (Google) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/dotprompt/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A7-S068: Promptfoo README (Promptfoo (OpenAI)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/promptfoo/promptfoo/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Ownership is moving here as elsewhere. ClickHouse announced its acquisition of Langfuse on 16 January 2026, and OpenAI announced its acquisition of Promptfoo on 9 March 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S075: Langfuse README (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; V2-S041: ClickHouse welcomes Langfuse (with langfuse.com 'Langfuse joins ClickHouse', Orrick) <https://clickhouse.com/blog/clickhouse-acquires-langfuse-open-source-llm-observability> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; V2-S042: OpenAI to acquire Promptfoo (with TechCrunch via Yahoo Finance, SDxCentral, Security Boulevard) <https://openai.com/blog/openai-to-acquire-promptfoo> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. The pitch is candid. Langfuse states that "prompt updates deploy instantly, without needing to involve engineering" [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S071: Langfuse docs: Prompt Management overview (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse-docs/main/content/docs/prompt-management/overview.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07]. That is genuinely useful for iteration, and dangerous for a regulated output unless the production label is protected [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**How it goes wrong.** The review's illustrative scenario is deliberately ordinary [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. During month-end, a well-meaning analyst edits a commentary prompt in a registry console and moves the production label. The edit also removes a sentence telling the model to state when an effect is below the materiality threshold. Forty commentaries are generated that evening, and several now describe immaterial currency effects as drivers of performance. Reviewers approve most of them because the numbers still match. When a portfolio manager later challenges one, the trace records the prompt's name but not its version, so the whole cycle has to be re-reviewed [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Four things would each have stopped or contained it: a protected production label, a pull request with a second approver, a style regression test, and a version on every trace [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The pattern, and where products fit.** The review recommends a hybrid [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Git is the record. CI runs every change against the regression suite, then publishes the approved version to the registry and moves the environment label using a release-pipeline identity. That identity is the only one allowed to move a protected production label. The application fetches by label, with a cached copy and a code-side default, and every trace carries the manifest version [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Each product supplies part of this, and each places it differently. Langfuse offers protected labels, an Enterprise feature when self-hosted [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S072: Langfuse docs: Prompt version control (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse-docs/main/content/docs/prompt-management/features/prompt-version-control.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A7-S074: Langfuse docs: Enterprise License Key (self-hosted) (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse-docs/main/content/self-hosting/license-key.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07]. LangSmith offers owners-only promotion, rollback history and synchronisation with a GitHub repository [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S076: LangSmith docs: Manage prompts (LangChain) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langchain-ai/docs/main/src/langsmith/manage-prompts.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07]. PromptLayer offers release labels for staged rollout [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S088: PromptLayer docs: Release Labels <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MagnivOrg/prompt-layer-docs/main/features/prompt-registry/release-labels.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07]. LaunchDarkly offers custom roles on Enterprise and a code-side default if the service is unreachable [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-C5-S001: Using SSO with LaunchDarkly; Configure SAML SSO; Creating custom roles <https://launchdarkly.com/docs/guides/account/sso> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]; A7-S089: LaunchDarkly Server-Side AI SDK for Python README <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/launchdarkly/python-server-sdk-ai/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Progressive rollout is valuable, but only between versions that have already passed approval and the evaluation gate. Rollout limits exposure; it does not replace approval [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Deciding what is material.** The post's leadership move rests on a classification the review proposes [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A wording change that passes the regression suite is a standard change. A model, embedding or tool change is a material change that triggers re-validation. A change of purpose goes to the model-risk owner [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Two regulatory facts sharpen that last line. SR 26-2 superseded SR 11-7 on 17 April 2026 and expressly excludes generative and agentic AI, so no US regulator defines prompt change control and the firm must set its own standard [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-US-MRM: Revised Guidance on Model Risk Management: Federal Reserve SR 26-2 / OCC Bulletin 2026-13 / FDIC FIL-15-2026 (supersedes SR 11-7 and SR 21-8) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S001: SR 26-2: Revised Guidance on Model Risk Management (17 April 2026) (Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System) <https://www.federalreserve.gov/supervisionreg/srletters/SR2602.htm> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A8-S002: OCC Bulletin 2026-13 Model Risk Management: Revised Guidance (attachment bulletin-2026-13a.pdf) (Office of the Comptroller of the Currency) <https://www.occ.gov/news-issuances/bulletins/2026/bulletin-2026-13.html> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Under Article 25 of the EU AI Act, a deployer becomes a provider if it changes an AI system's intended purpose so that it becomes high-risk [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-EUAIA: Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (Artificial Intelligence Act), as amended by Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (Digital Omnibus on AI) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S011: Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (Digital Omnibus on AI) amending Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (EUR-Lex / Official Journal of the EU) <https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2026/1744/oj/eng> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. A system prompt is the easiest place to make that change. A prompt edit that extends a commentary tool into, say, HR screening is a regulatory event, not a wording change [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The equivalent of effective challenge here is a second approver, ideally the risk owner for the use case [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Resilience and exit.** A configuration service on the request path is a new availability dependency [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Cached prompts and code-side defaults turn an outage into a degraded mode, and the default must itself be an approved version: a stale hard-coded default is an unreviewed configuration [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S071: Langfuse docs: Prompt Management overview (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse-docs/main/content/docs/prompt-management/overview.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A7-S089: LaunchDarkly Server-Side AI SDK for Python README <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/launchdarkly/python-server-sdk-ai/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A SaaS registry that delivers prompts to production is an ICT third-party service for the DORA register [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DORA: Regulation (EU) 2022/2554 Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S021: Digital Operational Resilience Act (DORA) pages and RoI reporting FAQ (ESMA / EBA / EIOPA) <https://www.esma.europa.eu/esmas-activities/digital-finance-and-innovation/digital-operational-resilience-act-dora> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Prompts held only in a vendor console must be re-created during a stressed exit, exactly when time is shortest [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. SS2/21 expects tested exit plans [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS221: PRA SS2/21 'Outsourcing and third party risk management' (revised by PS7/26, March 2026) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S048: SS2/21 Outsourcing and third party risk management (and PS7/26, March 2026) (Bank of England / PRA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/-/media/boe/files/prudential-regulation/supervisory-statement/2021/ss221-march-21.pdf> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Portable prompts and configuration, together with the gateway and the evaluation suite, are what make an exit from a model vendor feasible [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**In the worked example.** The commentary agent's release manifest pins, for one monthly cycle, the prompt set version, the drafting and fallback models, the embedding and reranker versions matching the index, top-k, the read-only tool list, the guardrail policy and the evaluation thresholds [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The values are illustrative. A second approver from investment risk is mandatory, and the release is frozen for the month-end cycle. Rollback means moving the label back to the previous release and re-running the affected funds [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### What good looks like

The signal is **rollback time**: from the decision to revert to the previous approved version serving all traffic [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The starting target is minutes, not a release cycle. Measure it in a rollback drill twice a year, from the decision timestamp to the point at which traces show the old version on every request [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Rollback is only meaningful if the versions are known, so three companion measures matter. **Configuration coverage** is the share of production calls whose prompt, model, tool and retrieval settings all resolve to an approved version; the starting target is 100% for regulated outputs [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. **Unapproved change rate**, meaning production changes without a recorded second approver, should be zero [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. **Trace-to-version linkage** should be at least 99.9% [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A fourth, **change lead time**, protects the teams: days, not weeks, for a standard change, and falling [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

These are starting points for a firm to calibrate, not industry benchmarks [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A drill that has never been run is a hope, however good the tooling [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 14: configuration.** A release manifest per monthly cycle (prompt set, model pins, retrieval settings, tool list, guard policy, eval thresholds), approved by pull request with a second approver and gated by the eval suite. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** Every setting that can change what a client reads is versioned and reversible. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** No production prompt is ever edited in a console. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** The manifest version stamped on every trace. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Rollback time, drilled. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"This will slow prompt iteration to a crawl."** It adds friction, and teams will feel it [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The answer is two paths. Experiments run freely on separate keys and non-production labels. Production changes go through the pull request and the evaluation gate [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Tracking change lead time keeps the cost visible, so the control does not quietly become a queue [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. If the slowest step turns out to be waiting for a reviewer, that is a staffing question to settle up front, not a reason to drop the review [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Our registry already versions prompts and has labels. Why add Git?"** Registry versioning is real and useful, and the pattern keeps the registry for run-time delivery [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. But change history and approvals held only in a vendor's audit log are model-risk evidence with a retention requirement, and the review classifies that as unacceptable lock-in [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Registries also change owners [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S075: Langfuse README (Langfuse (ClickHouse)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/langfuse/langfuse/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; V2-S041: ClickHouse welcomes Langfuse (with langfuse.com 'Langfuse joins ClickHouse', Orrick) <https://clickhouse.com/blog/clickhouse-acquires-langfuse-open-source-llm-observability> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. With Git as the record, the registry becomes a cache that can be rebuilt [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"A wording tweak is not a model change."** Usually it is not, and the classification says so: a wording change that passes the regression suite is a standard change [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The scenario above shows why the suite still has to run. One deleted sentence changed how materiality was reported [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. And some wording changes alter purpose, which is a different kind of event altogether [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The point of classifying in advance is that nobody has to make that judgement at month-end, under time pressure, about their own edit [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### Questions for your team

- Could one person write, approve and release a production prompt change today?
- For any output from last month, can we name the exact manifest that produced it?
- Which settings besides the prompt can change what a client reads, and are they versioned together?
- How long did our last rollback take, and when did we last drill it?
- Where does our approval history live, and would it survive a change of vendor?
- Who decides whether a configuration change is material, and is that written down?
- What happens to a production run if the configuration service is unreachable? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### In one line

Treat every setting that can change what a client reads as production configuration, with one record, a second approver and a rehearsed way back [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.


## 15. Many "hallucinations" start in a table parser

*L8 Data extraction and ingestion, from Post 15 of the series.*

### The post

Some of the most convincing "hallucinations" in enterprise retrieval systems are not the model's fault. They start in a table parser. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

A merged header cell shifts a column one place to the left. Parsing succeeds, chunks are indexed, retrieval works, and the model faithfully repeats a figure that was never the figure it claimed to be. Nothing fails, so nobody looks. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Ingestion is usually drawn as a shelf of parsers. The parsers are close to commodity now; the value is in the envelope around them. Every chunk should carry its source, version, access permissions, classification, the parser and version that produced it, and a lineage run ID. No envelope, no index. Of the ten ingestion products assessed in this review, only one carried access-control metadata into the pipeline, and none emitted lineage. The envelope is something you build. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S094: unstructured-ingest CHANGELOG (Unstructured Technologies (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unstructured-IO/unstructured-ingest/main/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A1-S096: OpenLineage README (OpenLineage (LF AI & Data)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenLineage/OpenLineage/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The signal worth watching is table cell exact-match against a golden set of your own documents: value, sign, unit, row and column. A starting target is 99.5%, and 100% for any table that feeds a published number, re-run on every parser or model change. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The leadership move is a boundary. Numbers parsed from documents are context, never the authority. Authoritative figures come from the system of record through a tool, and the evaluation checks the draft against that, not against parsed text. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The honest caveat: dual parsing and reconciliation cost compute and engineering time, and every parser upgrade becomes a tested change. That is the price of being able to say which documents a defect touched. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

If retrieved answers look wrong, look upstream first. The model is often just the messenger. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

![Many "hallucinations" start in a table parser](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P15.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 15. The parser is a replaceable cartridge; the value is the envelope around it. No envelope, no index, and parsed numbers are never the authority. Generic pipeline.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

Ingestion owns the path from an approved source to an indexed, governed unit of content: acquisition, parsing, OCR, structure extraction, cleaning, chunking, metadata and indexing [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. It does two different jobs that are easy to blur. One is acquisition: getting bytes from a source the firm is entitled to use. The other is document understanding: turning those bytes into faithful structure, with text, reading order, tables and their coordinates [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The first is a question of law, licences and entitlements. The second is a question of fidelity. Both are decided before any model sees a word.

**Why failures here look like failures somewhere else.** Ingestion failures surface as model failures [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A merged table cell becomes a "hallucinated" number. A permission that never reached the index becomes a data leak. A web page with hidden instructions becomes an indirect prompt injection. A parser upgrade silently shifts chunk boundaries [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The review's illustrative case is a fund factsheet whose "relative" column shifts one place left after a routine library upgrade. The commentary agent quotes last quarter's relative return, which is really the benchmark return. Nothing in the pipeline records which parser version produced the chunk, so nobody can tell which other documents are affected, and a quarter's corpus has to be re-parsed and re-reviewed by hand [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The cause is an unpinned parser and the absence of any table-reconciliation check. It is not the language model [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The parsers have become replaceable.** The market moved quickly in 2025–26. Docling, MIT-licensed and started at IBM Research, graduated within LF AI & Data in August 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S057: Docling README (Docling Project (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/docling-project/docling/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; V1-S091: Docling - LF AI & Data project page; LF press release 29 April 2025 (IBM contributes Docling, Data Prep Kit, BeeAI) (LF AI & Data Foundation) <https://lfaidata.foundation/projects/docling/> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. LlamaParse is now the name of LlamaIndex's whole document platform [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S080: LlamaIndex README (company focus shift to LlamaParse) (LlamaIndex, Inc. (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/run-llama/llama_index/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; V1-S008: LlamaParse (formerly LlamaCloud) (LlamaIndex) <https://developers.llamaindex.ai/python/framework/llama_cloud/> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Mistral released OCR 4.1 and retired OCR 4.0 on 30 September 2026, about five weeks after 4.1 became generally available [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S130: OCR 4.1 - Mistral AI docs <https://docs.mistral.ai/models/ocr-4-1> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V1-S014: Mistral docs Changelog; OCR 4.0 model page; OCR 4 governance page (Mistral AI) <https://docs.mistral.ai/resources/changelogs> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. Firecrawl's server is AGPL-3.0, with its enterprise controls offered only in its cloud service [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S053: Firecrawl README (Firecrawl (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/firecrawl/firecrawl/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A1-S079: Open source or Firecrawl Cloud (docs source) (Firecrawl (GitHub docs source)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/firecrawl/firecrawl-docs/main/contributing/open-source-or-cloud.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07]. Published prices differ by more than an order of magnitude: Google Document AI's Enterprise OCR at US$1.50 per 1,000 pages, Mistral OCR 4.1 at US$4 and Reducto Parse at US$10, as of 7 October 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S101: Document AI pricing (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/document-ai/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07; A1-S130: OCR 4.1 - Mistral AI docs <https://docs.mistral.ai/models/ocr-4-1> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A1-S113: Reducto Pricing <https://reducto.ai/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Two lessons follow. Engines are interchangeable enough to route by document class [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. And version churn is now an operational risk: a hosted model that has been retired cannot reproduce last quarter's parse, so parsed outputs and their manifests must be stored, not just raw documents [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The envelope is where the products stop.** Of the ten products assessed, only Unstructured's open-source connectors carry access-control metadata into the pipeline [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S094: unstructured-ingest CHANGELOG (Unstructured Technologies (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unstructured-IO/unstructured-ingest/main/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. They compute a digest of the access list at index time, reprocess a document when its permissions alone change, and record a failed permission fetch separately from an empty one [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S094: unstructured-ingest CHANGELOG (Unstructured Technologies (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unstructured-IO/unstructured-ingest/main/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. That last distinction is what stops a failed lookup being read as "public" [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. No product in the layer emits lineage [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S094: unstructured-ingest CHANGELOG (Unstructured Technologies (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unstructured-IO/unstructured-ingest/main/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A1-S096: OpenLineage README (OpenLineage (LF AI & Data)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenLineage/OpenLineage/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. OpenLineage, the neutral lineage standard, has no GenAI-specific facets, so parse, chunk, embed and index steps must be modelled as generic jobs or custom facets [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S042: OpenLineage CHANGELOG (OpenLineage (LF AI & Data)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenLineage/OpenLineage/main/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Apache Airflow ships an OpenLineage provider, so an Airflow-run pipeline can emit lineage without new tooling [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S045: apache-airflow-providers-openlineage on PyPI (PyPI / Apache Software Foundation) <https://pypi.org/pypi/apache-airflow-providers-openlineage/json> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. And no product reconciles parsed tables against an authoritative source; that has to be built [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

The review's verdict on ingestion is to keep it as a layer but change its scope: the enterprise additions are a control envelope to build around replaceable parsers, not features to buy [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The envelope has six parts [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. An approved-source register is the single entry point. An acquisition contract requires every connector, bought or built, to emit a content hash, source version and permission snapshot, and to fail closed when permissions cannot be fetched. Classification and data-loss prevention run before parsing, so they decide which engines a document may reach. A parse manifest records engine, version, model ID and configuration. A metadata envelope on every chunk is enforced as an index gate. Lineage events and incremental indexing on content hash and permission digest complete it, with deletions propagated to every store [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Policy comes from the privacy control (Chapter 16); evidence flows to governance (Chapter 18) [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The boundary that matters most.** The envelope tells you where a chunk came from. It does not make a parsed number true. In the review's worked example every figure comes from the attribution engine through read-only tools, every numeric cell parsed from a document is tagged as document-derived, and the numeric-faithfulness evaluation checks the draft against the engine output, not against parsed text [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A parsing error must never be able to become a published figure [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Where a parsed table does feed a number, dual-parse it with two engines and reconcile it against the stored authoritative output for that period, quarantining any mismatch [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The regulated reading.** ESMA expects "ex-ante input controls and frequent ex-post output controls" for AI used in investment services [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-INTL-AI-ASSETMGMT: IOSCO Supervisory Toolkit for AI Use in Capital Markets (FR/02/2026) and ESMA Public Statement on AI and investment services (30 May 2024) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S058: IOSCO FR/02/2026 Supervisory Toolkit for AI Use in Capital Markets (May 2026) <https://www.iosco.org/library/pubdocs/pdf/IOSCOPD823.pdf> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A8-S059: ESMA Public Statement on AI and investment services (30 May 2024) and 2026 briefings <https://www.esma.europa.eu/document/public-statement-ai-and-investment-services> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. In a retrieval system, ingestion is where the input controls live [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Parsers are not models in the SS1/23 or SR 26-2 sense, but they are data-preparation components whose errors flow straight into a model's inputs [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. SS1/23 is technology-agnostic and covers vendor models [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-PRA-SS123: PRA Supervisory Statement SS1/23 'Model risk management principles for banks' (published with PS6/23) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S008: SS1/23 Model risk management principles for banks (Bank of England / PRA) <https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/prudential-regulation/publication/2023/may/model-risk-management-principles-for-banks-ss> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]], so the cleanest course is to record parser and OCR engine versions as dependencies of each AI use case in the inventory [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Residency is decided here too: once a document has gone to a third-party parser and been indexed without its permissions, no prompt-level control can undo that [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The trade-off.** None of this is free. Dual parsing costs compute. Pinning versions turns every upgrade into a tested change. A golden set of house documents with hand-keyed tables takes analyst time to build and maintain [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The return is the ability to answer, within hours rather than weeks, which documents and which outputs a defect touched [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### What good looks like

The signal is **table cell exact-match**: the share of numeric table cells reproduced exactly, checking value, sign, unit, row and column [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Measure it against a golden set of your own factsheets and reports with hand-keyed tables, and re-run it on every parser, OCR engine or model change, so a regression is caught before it reaches the index [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

A reasonable starting target is at least 99.5% on the golden set, and 100% for any table that feeds a published number [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Treat these as a starting point to calibrate against your own documents, not as an industry benchmark.

Two companion measures keep the envelope honest [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Envelope completeness, the share of indexed chunks carrying source, version, permissions, classification, parser manifest and lineage ID, should be 100%, enforced as a hard gate at index time. Permission propagation lag, the time from a permission change at source to the index reflecting it, should start under an hour for confidential sources and under a day otherwise, tested with synthetic permission-change probes [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Report all three to the same dashboard as model quality, so upstream defects are not mistaken for model drift [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 15: ingestion.** Three corpora ingested under a control envelope: dual-parsed factsheet tables reconciled to the engine, prior commentaries with their permissions, and market notes from the approved register only. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** Every chunk carries its source, version, permissions, classification and lineage. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** No envelope, no index; parsed numbers are context only, never figures. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** Lineage IDs for every retrieved chunk. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Table cell exact-match on a golden set. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"Our parser vendor publishes strong accuracy figures."** It may well. But a vendor's accuracy claim is a reported claim measured on someone else's documents. The review records one such statement, a claim of twice the table accuracy and far less invented content than the open-source library, as reported only and not a decision input [R]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Reported — A1-S075: Unstructured open-source library README (Unstructured Technologies (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unstructured-IO/unstructured/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The test that counts is your golden set, on your layouts, with your merged headers and footnotes [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. It costs less to build than one manual re-review of a quarter's corpus.

**"An envelope we build ourselves is a product we will have to maintain."** True, and it should be kept thin. Most of it is metadata discipline and an index rule, not new software [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. It rests on open standards: OpenLineage for lineage, with Airflow's provider already available [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A7-S041: OpenLineage README (OpenLineage (LF AI & Data)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenLineage/OpenLineage/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A7-S045: apache-airflow-providers-openlineage on PyPI (PyPI / Apache Software Foundation) <https://pypi.org/pypi/apache-airflow-providers-openlineage/json> accessed 2026-10-07]. And the alternative is waiting for a product that does not yet exist; on current evidence no ingestion product emits lineage [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S094: unstructured-ingest CHANGELOG (Unstructured Technologies (GitHub)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unstructured-IO/unstructured-ingest/main/CHANGELOG.md> accessed 2026-10-07; A1-S096: OpenLineage README (OpenLineage (LF AI & Data)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenLineage/OpenLineage/main/README.md> accessed 2026-10-07].

**"Dual parsing doubles the cost of ingestion."** Only if it is applied everywhere. Reserve it for tables that feed numbers, and route everything else by document class, using a cheap OCR tier for simple scans and expensive tiers only for complex layouts [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. For most corpora the reconciled subset is small, and the alternative cost is a figure in a client document that nobody can trace [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. A second engine also earns its keep elsewhere: it is a qualified fallback when the first is retired, which on the 2026 evidence can happen within weeks of a successor's release [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A1-S130: OCR 4.1 - Mistral AI docs <https://docs.mistral.ai/models/ocr-4-1> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V1-S014: Mistral docs Changelog; OCR 4.0 model page; OCR 4 governance page (Mistral AI) <https://docs.mistral.ai/resources/changelogs> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### Questions for your team

- Which of our indexed corpora can tell us, for any chunk, which parser and which version produced it? [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Do we have a golden set of our own documents with hand-keyed tables, and when was it last run? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Does any connector we use treat a failed permission fetch as "no restrictions"? [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Which hosted parsing or OCR engines do we depend on, and what is their retirement notice period against our re-validation cycle? [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Can any number parsed from a document reach a published output without being checked against the system of record? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- Is classification applied before a document reaches a third-party engine, or after it is indexed? [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### In one line

Treat the parser as a replaceable cartridge, build the envelope around it, and never let a parsed number become the authority. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}


## 16. One client identifier, seven copies to protect

*C3 DLP and PII protection, from Post 16 of the series.*

### The post

One analyst question can place the same client identifier in seven places: a prompt, a retrieved chunk, a tool result, a provider's processing region, a trace store, an evaluation dataset and a memory record. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Each copy has its own retention, location and access model. Each is somewhere an erasure request or a breach investigation has to reach. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The common trap is a single checkpoint: a filter at the prompt. By then the document may already have been parsed by a third party and indexed without its classification, and no prompt-level control can undo that. Ingestion is where data quality is decided. It is also where residency and privacy are decided. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The pattern that holds up is one firm-owned privacy service — detect, transform and, under policy, re-identify — called from every enforcement point: ingestion, prompts, tool results, outputs, memory writes and trace export. One policy, six call sites, rather than a different detector bought for each layer. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The signal is detection recall per entity class, measured on a labelled test set of your own identifiers: client codes, account numbers, mandate references. Report it per class, not averaged. An average hides the identifier that matters. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The leadership move is to fund tokenisation, not blunt redaction. "[REDACTED] outperformed [REDACTED]" gets the control switched off. Consistent placeholders let the model write a useful draft without ever seeing who the client is. [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

The honest caveat: detectors miss things, and the leading open-source one says so in its own documentation. Design for a second-pass scan and a residual-leakage measure, not a perfect first pass. [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S068: Presidio README (GitHub, data-privacy-stack) (Presidio / Data Privacy Stack) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/data-privacy-stack/presidio/main/README.MD> accessed 2026-10-07] [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

Privacy controls fail quietly when they sit in one place. They hold when data cannot move without passing them. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

![One client identifier, seven copies to protect](Enterprise_GenAI_Stack_Oct2026/08_Graphic/linkedin/P16.png){width=4.2in}

*Figure 16. A filter at the prompt arrives too late. One privacy service, called at six enforcement points, protects every copy. Illustrative: placeholders, not names.* [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Behind the post

This control has one job: making sure personal data and confidential business data reach a model, a store or a third party only in the form the firm's policy allows for that destination [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. It breaks into five tasks. Classification knows which sources and fields are sensitive before they are used. Detection finds sensitive entities at run time. Transformation redacts, masks, generalises or tokenises them so the next step still works. Controlled re-identification reverses a token only for an entitled person and a recorded purpose. And residency and evidence keep each copy in an approved place and record what happened [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Why copies are the problem.** Generative AI multiplies copies of data [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The seven places in the post are not hypothetical; they are the normal path of one request through a retrieval-augmented agent. The control fails in four recognisable ways [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Detectors tuned for generic personal data miss the identifiers that matter in a fund manager: internal client codes, account numbers, mandate references. Placement at a single point, usually the gateway, leaves ingestion, memory and traces carrying raw data. Blunt redaction removes what the model needs, so teams switch the control off. And re-identification is done by whoever holds a key, with no recorded purpose [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. In an asset manager the most sensitive content is often not personal at all: holdings, mandate terms and unpublished performance, none of which a personal-data detector looks for [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**One service, six call sites.** The review places the privacy service at six enforcement points [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. At ingestion it classifies and detects before parsing, and tags every chunk (Chapter 15). At prompt egress, in the gateway, it tokenises before any external model call. On tool results it transforms client data before it enters the context window. On model output it inspects the response, including leakage from retrieval or memory, and restores placeholders only for entitled users. On memory writes it stops raw identifiers reaching long-term memory. On telemetry it redacts at the firm-owned collector before traces leave the estate [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The policy is written once and enforced six times [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**How transformation keeps text useful.** Traditional data-loss prevention blocks or masks. Generative AI needs transformations that keep text useful to the model and reversible for the reviewer, plus a second inspection of the output, because a model can reproduce sensitive data from retrieval, memory or its training [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The method depends on two questions: does the model need the value, and must anyone reverse it? Values the model never needs are redacted. Identities the model must keep distinct are replaced with consistent placeholders, CLIENT_A and MANDATE_1, which a reviewer later sees restored [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The products support this in different ways. Google Sensitive Data Protection offers masking, tokenisation and bucketing [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S066: Sensitive Data Protection product page (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/sensitive-data-protection> accessed 2026-10-07]. Protegrity offers policy-based protect and unprotect [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S082: protegrity-developer-python - PyPI JSON metadata (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/protegrity-developer-python/json> accessed 2026-10-07]. Skyflow offers vault tokens with authorised rehydration [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S081: skyflow - PyPI JSON metadata (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/skyflow/json> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S095: Skyflow GDPR Compliance; Data Privacy Vault; LLM Privacy Vault; SOC 2 solution pages; Why Skyflow (docs) <https://www.skyflow.com/gdpr-compliance> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. After the model returns, placeholders are checked, none altered and none invented, before anything is restored [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Three trust models.** The engines behind the service are not interchangeable in one important respect. An in-estate library such as Presidio keeps text inside the firm's boundary [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S068: Presidio README (GitHub, data-privacy-stack) (Presidio / Data Privacy Stack) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/data-privacy-stack/presidio/main/README.MD> accessed 2026-10-07]. A managed API such as Sensitive Data Protection sends text to the cloud provider and is billed per gibibyte inspected: US$3.00 after the first free gibibyte, falling to US$2.00 above a tebibyte, as of 7 October 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S065: Sensitive Data Protection pricing (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/sensitive-data-protection/pricing> accessed 2026-10-07]. A vault such as Skyflow stores the sensitive values with the vendor and returns tokens [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S081: skyflow - PyPI JSON metadata (Python Package Index) <https://pypi.org/pypi/skyflow/json> accessed 2026-10-07]. The choice is a residency decision before it is a feature decision [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**What changed in 2025–26.** Presidio is no longer a Microsoft project; it is community-governed under the Data Privacy Stack organisation and MIT-licensed [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S040: Presidio has transitioned to a community-owned project (project_transition.md) (Presidio / Data Privacy Stack) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/data-privacy-stack/presidio/main/docs/project_transition.md> accessed 2026-10-07; V2-S030: Presidio has transitioned to a community-owned project (Presidio / Data Privacy Stack) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/data-privacy-stack/presidio/main/docs/project_transition.md> accessed 2026-10-08]. Microsoft folded its AI posture tooling into a unified Purview Data Security Posture Management, generally available in May 2026 and requiring Microsoft 365 E5 or the Purview Suite [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S090: What's new in Microsoft Purview <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/purview/whats-new> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S091: Considerations for deploying Microsoft Purview DSPM for AI; Get started with DSPM (classic); Purview for Microsoft Foundry <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/purview/dspm-for-ai-considerations> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Google's Sensitive Data Protection underpins Model Armor's screening of prompts and responses [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S066: Sensitive Data Protection product page (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/sensitive-data-protection> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S067: Model Armor product page (incl. pricing) (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/model-armor> accessed 2026-10-07]. Protegrity's AI Team Edition is still documented as Tech Preview and runs on AWS only [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S093: Protegrity AI Team Edition to Secure Agentic Workflows; AI Team Edition docs (Tech Preview); Protegrity Expands Team (April 2026) <https://www.protegrity.com/news/protegrity-ai-team-edition-to-secure-agentic-workflows> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Meanwhile, sensitive-data inspection now appears inside Cloudflare AI Gateway, Kong AI Gateway, Bedrock Guardrails and Model Armor [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S052: Cloudflare AI Gateway docs: Pricing (source mdx) (Cloudflare, Inc.) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/production/src/content/docs/ai-gateway/reference/pricing.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S016: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA — And It's Already Moving Faster (Kong Inc.) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S072: AWS API model: Amazon Bedrock control plane (botocore service-2.json) - guardrail shapes (Amazon Web Services (via boto/botocore)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/boto/botocore/develop/botocore/data/bedrock/2023-04-20/service-2.json> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S067: Model Armor product page (incl. pricing) (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/model-armor> accessed 2026-10-07]. That is useful, and it is also the risk: a different detector and policy per product is the pattern the review calls out as an anti-pattern [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**Location became a contract term.** Processing location is now something firms configure and sometimes pay for. One first-party model API, Anthropic's, offers a US-only inference option at 1.1 times the price [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DATA-TRANSFERS: UK GDPR as amended by the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025; EU adequacy decisions for the UK (GDPR and LED); EU-US Data Privacy Framework (Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2023/1795) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S036: Data residency (Claude Platform docs) (Anthropic) <https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/data-residency.md> accessed 2026-10-07]. Independent alternatives exist on every major cloud: Bedrock's geographic profiles keep EU-originated requests within EU Regions [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S005: Geographic cross-Region inference - Amazon Bedrock (Amazon Web Services) <https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/geographic-cross-region-inference.html> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]], Microsoft Foundry's Data Zone deployments process only within the US or EU zone [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S006: Understanding deployment types in Microsoft Foundry Models <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/foundry-models/concepts/deployment-types> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]], and Google Cloud's regional endpoints keep processing within a jurisdiction [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — B-L2-S008: Data residency (with Deployments and endpoints; Single Zone Provisioned Throughput) - Vertex AI (Google Cloud) <https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/learn/data-residency> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. The point is the same across all four: the endpoint, the trace store, the memory store and the privacy service itself each need their own residency setting [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**The regulated reading.** In the UK, the regulator states that all the data protection provisions of the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025 were in force as of 19 June 2026 [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DATA-TRANSFERS: UK GDPR as amended by the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025; EU adequacy decisions for the UK (GDPR and LED); EU-US Data Privacy Framework (Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2023/1795) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S051: Data (Use and Access) Act 2025: commencement (GOV.UK, SI 2025/904, SI 2026/82, ICO) (DSIT / legislation.gov.uk / ICO) <https://www.gov.uk/guidance/data-use-and-access-act-2025-plans-for-commencement> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; V2-S076: The Data (Use and Access) Act 2025 - commencement dates and planned guidance for 2026 (Kennedys; with Stephenson Harwood tracker, A&O Shearman) <https://www.kennedyslaw.com/en/thought-leadership/article/2026/the-data-use-and-access-act-2025-commencement-dates-and-planned-guidance-for-2026> accessed 2026-10-08 [search-tool extract]]. The EU–US Data Privacy Framework remains in effect, but an appeal against it, C-703/25 P, is pending [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-DATA-TRANSFERS: UK GDPR as amended by the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025; EU adequacy decisions for the UK (GDPR and LED); EU-US Data Privacy Framework (Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2023/1795) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S053: Case C-703/25 P Latombe v Commission (appeal against T-553/23) (EUR-Lex / Court of Justice of the EU) <https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/C/2025/6610/oj/eng> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]. Tokenising identifiers before a US-hosted model call reduces the personal data exposed to that transfer risk [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. ESMA expects ex-ante input controls and ex-post output controls [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — R-INTL-AI-ASSETMGMT: IOSCO Supervisory Toolkit for AI Use in Capital Markets (FR/02/2026) and ESMA Public Statement on AI and investment services (30 May 2024) (see regulatory_facts.json); A8-S059: ESMA Public Statement on AI and investment services (30 May 2024) and 2026 briefings <https://www.esma.europa.eu/document/public-statement-ai-and-investment-services> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]]; prompt-side tokenisation is the input control and output inspection is the output control [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. One caution: tokenised data is still personal data in the records of processing, because the firm can reverse it [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### What good looks like

The signal is **detection recall per entity class**: the share of seeded sensitive entities the detectors find, reported separately for client names, account numbers, internal client codes, mandate references, contact details and other classes [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Measure it on a labelled test corpus of your own identifiers, held in version control and run in CI on every detector, recogniser or model change [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

A reasonable starting target is at least 0.98 for identifiers that must never leave the estate, reported per class and never averaged [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Treat it as a starting point to calibrate, not a benchmark; a class with low precision will drive users to bypass the control, so track precision alongside it [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

Three companions complete the picture [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The residual sensitive-data rate, found by an independent second-pass scanner on sampled chunks, prompts and traces, should be zero for classes that must be masked. Enforcement-point coverage, reconciled from gateway and collector logs, should be 100% for external egress. Placeholder integrity, outputs in which the model altered, invented or dropped a placeholder, should allow none into final text [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

### In the worked example

**Step 16: privacy.** One privacy service called at six points: client names and mandates tokenised before any model call, detected again in output, re-identified only for the reviewing manager. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

- **Now works:** The model writes about 'the mandate' without ever seeing whose it is. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Must never:** A clear-text identifier not in the approved inputs blocks the draft. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Evidence added:** The tokenised prompt and the token-map reference, never clear identifiers. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}
- **Signal:** Identifier leakage in traces and outputs: zero. [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}

### Objections worth taking seriously

**"Our gateway already inspects prompts."** Good: keep it, as one call site. A gateway sees prompts and responses, but not the document that was parsed by a third party last month, the memory record written yesterday or the trace exported overnight [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. The review's own counter-evidence is that inspection is moving into gateways and guardrails [VF]{custom-style="Claim Label"}^[Verified fact — A6-S052: Cloudflare AI Gateway docs: Pricing (source mdx) (Cloudflare, Inc.) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-docs/production/src/content/docs/ai-gateway/reference/pricing.mdx> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S016: Kong AI Gateway 2.0 Is Now GA — And It's Already Moving Faster (Kong Inc.) <https://konghq.com/blog/product-releases/kong-ai-gateway-2-0-ga> accessed 2026-10-07 [search-tool extract]; A6-S072: AWS API model: Amazon Bedrock control plane (botocore service-2.json) - guardrail shapes (Amazon Web Services (via boto/botocore)) <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/boto/botocore/develop/botocore/data/bedrock/2023-04-20/service-2.json> accessed 2026-10-07; A6-S067: Model Armor product page (incl. pricing) (Google Cloud) <https://cloud.google.com/security/products/model-armor> accessed 2026-10-07]. The answer is to have those components call the same policy, not to let each carry its own [Rec]{custom-style="Claim Label"}. Otherwise the firm ends up with four definitions of a client identifier, four sets of test results and no single answer when an auditor asks which one applied to a given request [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.

**"Tokenisation is complicated. Redaction is simpler."** It is simpler to build and harder to live with. Redaction that strips the client's name also strips the sentence's meaning, and the control gets switched off [AJ]{custom-style="Claim Label"}.
